  <tr>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">1</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">Less than 51 mm</td>
  <td class="noBorderBottom">0.08 m<sup>3</sup></td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">2</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">51 mm or more but less than
  101 mm</td>
  <td class="noBorderBottom">0.16 m<sup>3</sup></td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">3</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">101 mm or more but less than
  153 mm</td>
  <td class="noBorderBottom">0.32 m<sup>3</sup></td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">4</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">153 mm or more but less than
  305 mm</td>
  <td class="noBorderBottom">0.48 m<sup>3</sup></td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
  <td class="noBorderRight">5</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight">305 mm or more</td>
  <td>0.64 m<sup>3</sup></td>
  </tr>
  </tbody>
  </table>
  <figcaption><p>TABLE</p></figcaption>
  </figure>

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Tanks for oily residue and sludge oil

**[[16]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-16 .sectionLabel}** The authorized
representative of a vessel of 400 gross tonnage or more must ensure that
it is fitted with tanks that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} have an adequate capacity, having regard to the type
  of machinery fitted on the vessel and the vessel's usual length of
  voyage, to receive the vessel's oily residues and sludge oil; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} are designed and constructed so as to facilitate
  their cleaning.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Oil fuel tank protection

- **[[17]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  authorized representative of a vessel that is delivered on or after
  1 August 2010, as defined in regulation 1.28.9 of Annex I to MARPOL,
  and has an aggregate oil fuel capacity of 600 m^3^ or more must ensure
  that the requirements of regulation 12A of that Annex are met.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Small oil fuel tanks

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply in respect of an oil
  fuel tank that has a capacity of 30 m^3^ or less if the aggregate
  capacity of all such tanks on the vessel is 600 m^3^ or less.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Forepeak tanks and tanks forward of the
collision bulkhead

**[[18]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18 .sectionLabel}** The authorized
representative and the master of a vessel of 400 gross tonnage or more
that is put into service after February 15, 1993 must ensure that it is
does not carry oil in a forepeak tank or in a tank forward of the
collision bulkhead.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cargo spaces in vessels other than oil
tankers

- **[[19]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-19 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  vessel, other than an oil tanker, is fitted with cargo spaces that are
  constructed and used for carrying oil in bulk and have an aggregate
  capacity of at least 200 m^3^,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the vessel's authorized representative must ensure
    that the requirements of regulation 26.4 of Annex I to MARPOL,
    subparagraphs 12(1)(a)(iv) to (vii), sections 15 and 18 and
    Subdivision 7 are met in respect of the vessel;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the vessel's master must ensure that the
    requirements of section 18 and subsection 40(7) are met in respect
    of the vessel; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the vessel must keep on board an equipment
    operation manual for its oil discharge monitoring and control system
    that meets the requirements of regulation 31.4 of Annex I to MARPOL.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- capacity of less than
  1 000 m^3^

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite paragraph (1)(a), if the cargo spaces have an
  aggregate capacity of less than 1 000 m^3^ and the vessel is equipped
  with installations that can retain on board contaminated cargo
  washings and cargo wastes for the purpose of their subsequent transfer
  to a reception facility, the vessel's authorized representative need
  not ensure that the requirements of subparagraphs 12(1)(a)(iv), (vi)
  and (vii) are met.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Pumps

- **[[20]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-20 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  authorized representative of a vessel of 400 gross tonnage or more
  that is fitted with main or auxiliary propulsion machinery must ensure
  that the vessel is equipped with one or more pumps that can transfer
  oily residues from its machinery space bilges and sludge oil tanks
  through a piping system to a reception facility.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Piping systems

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The vessel's authorized representative must ensure
  that the piping system has

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a stop valve that is accessible from the weather
    deck; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an outlet that is accessible from the weather deck
    and is fitted with a standard discharge connection that meets the
    requirements of regulation 13 of Annex I to MARPOL.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Connections overboard

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The vessel's authorized representative must ensure
  that the piping to and from sludge oil tanks has no direct connection
  overboard other than the standard discharge connection.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Means for stopping discharge pumps

**[[21]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-21 .sectionLabel}** The authorized
representative of a Canadian vessel, or a Canadian pleasure craft, of
400 gross tonnage or more that is fitted with main or auxiliary
propulsion machinery must ensure that the vessel is equipped on the
weather deck with a means for stopping each pump that is used to
discharge oily residues and sludge oil.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Retention or discharge equipment --- oil
tankers of 150 gross tonnage or less

- **[[22]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-22 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  authorized representative of an oil tanker of 150 gross tonnage or
  less must ensure that it is equipped with

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} installations that can retain on board oily
    residues, contaminated cargo washings and cargo wastes for the
    purpose of their subsequent transfer to a reception facility; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} equipment that meets the oily mixture discharge
    requirements of section 30 or 31, as the case may be.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Retention or discharge equipment ---
  vessels of less than 400 gross tonnage

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The authorized representative of a vessel of less
  than 400 gross tonnage that carries oil as fuel or as cargo must
  ensure that the vessel is equipped with

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} installations that can retain on board oily
    residues for the purpose of their subsequent transfer to a reception
    facility; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} equipment that meets the oily mixture discharge
    requirements of section 30 or 31, as the case may be.

#### [SUBDIVISION 2]{.HLabel3}[Certificates, Endorsements and Inspections]{.HTitleText3} {#h-790040 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Issuance of Canadian Oil Pollution
Prevention Certificates

- **[[23]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-23 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On
  application by the authorized representative of a Canadian vessel or a
  Canadian pleasure craft and subject to paragraphs 16(4)(b) to (d) of
  the Act, the Minister must issue a Canadian Oil Pollution Prevention
  Certificate to the vessel if the applicable requirements of this
  Division are met.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Issuance of International Oil Pollution
  Prevention Certificates

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On application by the authorized representative of a
  Canadian vessel or a Canadian pleasure craft and subject to
  paragraphs 16(4)(b) to (d) of the Act, the Minister must issue an
  International Oil Pollution Prevention Certificate to the vessel if
  the applicable requirements of Annex I to MARPOL are met.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Endorsement of Canadian Oil Pollution
Prevention Certificates

- **[[24]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-24 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  authorized representative of a vessel that holds a Canadian Oil
  Pollution Prevention Certificate must ensure that the certificate is
  endorsed by the Minister, within three months before or after each
  anniversary date of the issuance of the certificate, to indicate that
  the requirements for the issuance of the certificate are met.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Endorsement of International Oil
  Pollution Prevention Certificates

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The authorized representative of a vessel that holds
  an International Oil Pollution Prevention Certificate must ensure that
  the certificate is endorsed as required by regulations 6.1.3, 6.1.4
  and 7.2 of Annex I to MARPOL.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inspection

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the construction, arrangement, equipment,
  fittings, installations or systems of a vessel that holds a
  certificate issued under section 23 are changed as a result of an
  accident, the discovery of a defect, a repair or a major conversion
  that affects the requirements that were met when the certificate was
  issued, the authorized representative of the vessel must ensure that
  the Minister inspects the vessel as soon as feasible to ensure that
  the requirements continue to be met.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (3) does not apply in respect of minor
  repairs or the direct replacement of equipment or fittings that meet
  the requirements of the certificate.

#### [SUBDIVISION 3]{.HLabel3}[Shipboard Documents]{.HTitleText3} {#h-790055 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificates

- **[[25]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-25 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every
  oil tanker of 150 gross tonnage or more, and every other vessel of
  400 gross tonnage or more, that carries oil as cargo or as fuel must
  hold and keep on board

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a Canadian Oil Pollution Prevention Certificate, if
    the vessel is a Canadian vessel or a Canadian pleasure craft and
    engages only on voyages in waters under Canadian jurisdiction;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an International Oil Pollution Prevention
    Certificate that is in the form set out in appendix II to Annex I to
    MARPOL, if the vessel

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is a Canadian vessel or a Canadian pleasure craft
      and does not engage only on voyages in waters under Canadian
      jurisdiction, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} is entitled to fly the flag of a foreign state
      that is a party to MARPOL; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a certificate of compliance certifying that the
    vessel meets the applicable requirements of Annex I to MARPOL, if
    the vessel is entitled to fly the flag of a state that is not a
    party to MARPOL.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificates of type approval, etc.

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every oil tanker of 150 gross tonnage or more, and
  every other vessel of 400 gross tonnage or more that carries oil as
  cargo or as fuel, must keep on board

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a copy of the certificate of type approval for any
    of the following equipment that is fitted on the vessel:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a 100 ppm oily-water separator and a process
      unit,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} oil filtering equipment,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} an oil content meter for the equipment referred
      to in subparagraph (i) or (ii),

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} an oil content meter for an oil discharge
      monitoring and control system, and

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} an oil-water interface detector;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in respect of the equipment referred to in
    subparagraphs (a)(iii) and (iv), a calibration certificate issued by
    or on behalf of its manufacturer;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an oil tanker, the information
    referred to in regulation 28.5 of Annex I to MARPOL relative to
    loading and distribution of cargo and the data referred to in that
    regulation on the ability of the tanker to comply with damage
    stability criteria;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an oil tanker fitted with an oil
    discharge monitoring and control system, an equipment operation
    manual that meets the requirements of regulation 31.4 of Annex I to
    MARPOL;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a crude oil tanker of 20 000 tonnes
    deadweight or more,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} an Operations and Equipment Manual for the
      tanker's crude oil washing system that meets the requirements of
      regulation 35.1 of Annex I to MARPOL and, if the tanker is a
      Canadian vessel, that is approved by the Minister as meeting those
      requirements, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} instruction manuals for the tanker's inert gas
      system that contain the information and operational instructions
      referred to in section 11 of Inert Gas Systems, 1990 edition,
      published by the IMO, and, if the tanker is a Canadian vessel,
      that are approved by the Minister as meeting the requirements of
      that section; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a combination carrier in respect of
    which the Minister or, in the case of a combination carrier that is
    a foreign vessel, the government of the state whose flag the carrier
    is entitled to fly has allowed simple supplementary operational
    procedures for liquid transfer operations under regulation 27.2 of
    Annex I to MARPOL, procedures that meet the requirements of
    regulation 27.3 of that Annex.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Subparagraph (2)(e)(ii)

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subparagraph (2)(e)(ii) applies

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} only in respect of an oil tanker delivered after
    1 June 1982 as defined in regulation 1.28.4 of Annex I to MARPOL;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in respect of Canadian vessels only in waters under
    Canadian jurisdiction.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Survey report file

- **[[26]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-26 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every
  oil tanker that is more than five years of age must keep on board the
  survey report file and supporting documentation, including the
  condition evaluation report, referred to in section 6 of the
  Guidelines on the Enhanced Programme of Inspections During Surveys of
  Bulk Carriers and Oil Tankers, Annex B to IMO Resolution A.744(18).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Age

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this section, the age of an oil
  tanker is determined from the day on which it is first delivered.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Language

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If an oil tanker engages on voyages only in waters
  under Canadian jurisdiction, the condition evaluation report must be
  in English or French.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Emergency plan

- **[[27]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-27
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (2) and (3),
  every oil tanker of 150 gross tonnage or more, and every other vessel
  of 400 gross tonnage or more that carries oil as cargo or as fuel,
  must keep on board a shipboard oil pollution emergency plan that meets
  the requirements of regulation 37 of Annex I to MARPOL.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A vessel that does not have mechanical means of
  propulsion and that is fitted with internal combustion engines having
  a total output of less than 400 kW does not require a shipboard oil
  pollution emergency plan unless it is carrying 10 tonnes or more of
  oil

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in bulk; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in tanks, if one or more of the tanks has a
    capacity greater than 450 L.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Subsection 57(1)

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If subsection 57(1) applies, the shipboard oil
  pollution emergency plan may be combined with the shipboard marine
  pollution emergency plan for noxious liquid substances, in which case
  the title of the plan must be the "shipboard marine pollution
  emergency plan".

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Damage stability and residual
  structural strength calculation

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An oil tanker of 5 000 tonnes deadweight or more must
  have prompt access to computerized, shore-based damage stability and
  residual structural strength calculation programs.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}STS operations Plan

- **[[27.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-27.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every oil tanker of 150 gross
  tonnage or more that is not alongside a wharf or quay and that is
  engaged with another oil tanker in a transfer operation involving oil
  or an oily mixture in bulk must keep on board an STS operations Plan
  that meets the requirements of regulation 41 of Annex I to MARPOL. In
  the case of a Canadian vessel, the STS operations Plan must be written
  in English or French or in both, according to the needs of the crew.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply in respect of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} transfer operations associated with fixed or
    floating platforms, including

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} drilling rigs,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} floating production, storage and off-loading
      facilities used for the offshore production and storage of oil,
      and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} floating storage units used for the offshore
      storage of produced oil;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} bunkering operations; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} transfer operations necessary for the purpose of
    saving lives or securing the safety of a vessel, or for combatting
    specific pollution incidents in order to minimize the damage from
    pollution.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2013-68, s. 5
:::

#### [SUBDIVISION 4]{.HLabel3}[Discharges of Oil and Oily Mixtures]{.HTitleText3} {#h-790114 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

**[[28]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-28 .sectionLabel}** This Subdivision does not
apply in respect of vessels in a shipping safety control zone or
Canadian vessels in an area in respect of which subsection 7(1) applies.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition

**[[29]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-29 .sectionLabel}** A Canadian vessel in
waters that are not waters under Canadian jurisdiction, and a person on
such a vessel, must not discharge oil or an oily mixture except in
accordance with section 31 or in the circumstances set out in section 5
that apply in respect of the discharge.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authorized discharge --- Section I waters

- **[[30]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-30 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purposes of section 187 of the Act, an oily mixture may be
  discharged from a vessel in Section I waters if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the vessel is en route;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} none of the oily mixture

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} originates in cargo pump room bilges, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} is mixed with oil cargo residues;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the discharge is processed through oil filtering
    equipment that

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} meets the requirements of regulation 14 of
      Annex I to MARPOL,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} produces an undiluted effluent that has an oil
      content of not more than 15 ppm, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} triggers an alarm and a discharge-stopping
      device as soon as the oil content in the effluent exceeds

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} 5 ppm, if the oily mixture is discharged in the
        inland waters of Canada, or

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} 15 ppm, if the oily mixture is discharged in
        Section I waters that do not include the inland waters of
        Canada; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the discharge does not contain chemicals or any
    other substances introduced for the purpose of circumventing the
    detection of concentrations of oil that exceed the oil content
    limits specified in subparagraph (c)(iii).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Alarms

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The alarm required for the purposes of
  clause (1)(c)(iii)(A) must meet the requirements of section 13 and the
  alarm required for the purposes of clause (1)(c)(iii)(B) must meet the
  requirements of regulation 14 of Annex I to MARPOL.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authorized discharge --- Section II
waters and seaward

- **[[31]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-31 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purposes of section 187 of the Act and section 29, an oily mixture
  may be discharged from a vessel in Section II waters or a Canadian
  vessel in waters that are not waters under Canadian jurisdiction if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the vessel is en route;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an oil tanker, none of the oily
    mixture

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} originates in cargo pump room bilges, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} is mixed with oil cargo residues;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the discharge is processed through oil filtering
    equipment that

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} meets the requirements of regulation 14 of
      Annex I to MARPOL, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} produces an undiluted effluent that has an oil
      content of not more than 15 ppm; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the discharge does not contain chemicals or any
    other substances introduced for the purpose of circumventing the
    detection of concentrations of oil that exceed the oil content limit
    specified in subparagraph (c)(ii).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authorized discharge from cargo spaces

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of section 187 of the Act and
  section 29, an oily mixture from cargo spaces may be discharged from a
  vessel in Section II waters or a Canadian vessel in waters that are
  not waters under Canadian jurisdiction if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the vessel is en route;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the vessel is more than 50 nautical miles from the
    nearest land;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the instantaneous rate of discharge of the oil that
    is in the oily mixture does not exceed 30 L per nautical mile;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the total quantity of oil discharged does not
    exceed

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} 1/15,000 of the cargo of which the oily mixture
      forms part, in the case of a vessel that was put into service on
      or before December 31, 1979,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} 1/30,000 of the cargo of which the oily mixture
      forms part, in the case of a vessel that is put into service after
      December 31, 1979, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} despite subparagraph (i), 1/30,000 of the cargo
      of which the oily mixture forms part, in the case of a Canadian
      vessel whose registration is transferred to the Register after
      February 15, 1993; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the oil discharge monitoring and control system is
    in operation and can stop the discharge of

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} any effluent having an oil discharge rate greater
      than that allowed under paragraph (c), or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} any oil in a quantity greater than that allowed
      under paragraph (d).

#### [SUBDIVISION 5]{.HLabel3}[Transfer Operations]{.HTitleText3} {#h-790158 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

- **[[32]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-32 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} This
  Subdivision applies in respect of vessels only when they are in waters
  under Canadian jurisdiction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Sections 33 and 37 and paragraphs 38(1)(b) to (d)
  and (f) to (j) do not apply in respect of oil tankers of less than
  150 gross tonnage or other vessels of less than 400 gross tonnage.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Sections 33, 34 and 37 and paragraphs 38(1)(b) to (d)
  and (g) to (i) do not apply in respect of an oil tanker without a
  master or crew and from which oil is being unloaded if the tanker is
  not attended by a vessel with a master or crew and the tanker is in a
  location that makes it infeasible to meet the requirements of those
  provisions.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application in waters that are not
  Canadian waters

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1),

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} paragraph 38(1)(l) also applies in respect of
    Canadian vessels in waters other than waters under Canadian
    jurisdiction; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} section 39.1 also applies in respect of Canadian
    vessels in the territorial sea or the exclusive economic zone of a
    foreign state that is a party to MARPOL.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2013-68, s. 6
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-32-20120330.html "Link to previous version of section 32"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Communications

**[[33]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-33 .sectionLabel}** If a vessel or a handling
facility engages in a transfer operation, the vessel's master and the
operator of the facility must, before and during the transfer operation,
have the means for two-way voice communication on a continuing basis
that enables the supervisor on board the vessel and the supervisor at
the facility or on board the other vessel

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} to communicate immediately as the need arises; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} to direct the immediate shutdown of the transfer
  operation in case of an emergency.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Lighting

- **[[34]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-34 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  vessel or a handling facility engages in a transfer operation between
  sunset and sunrise, the vessel's master and the operator of the
  facility must ensure that illumination is provided that has

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a lighting intensity of not less than 54 lx at each
    transfer connection point of the vessel or facility; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a lighting intensity of not less than 11 lx at each
    transfer operation work area around each transfer connection point
    of the vessel or facility.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Measurement

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (1), lighting
  intensity is to be measured on a horizontal plane 1 m above the
  walking surface of the facility or the working deck of the vessel, as
  applicable.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Transfer conduits

- **[[35]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-35 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person must not use a transfer conduit in a transfer operation unless
  the conduit

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} has a bursting pressure of not less than four times
    its maximum design pressure;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is clearly marked with its maximum design pressure;
    and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} has successfully passed, during the year before its
    use, a hydrostatic test to a pressure equal to one and one-half
    times its maximum design pressure.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Test certificates

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a transfer conduit used in a transfer operation is
  part of a vessel's equipment, the vessel's master must keep on board
  the test certificate for the hydrostatic test.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Manufacturer's specifications

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The owner of a transfer conduit that is used in a
  transfer operation must ensure that the conduit is used, maintained,
  tested and replaced in accordance with the manufacturer's
  specifications.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Leaks

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If a transfer conduit or a connection leaks during a
  transfer operation, the supervisor on board the vessel and the
  supervisor at the handling facility or on board the other vessel must,
  as soon as feasible, slow down or stop the operation to remove the
  pressure from the conduit or connection.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reception facility --- standard discharge
connections

**[[36]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-36 .sectionLabel}** The owner of a reception
facility that receives oily residues from a vessel's machinery space
bilges or sludge oil tanks must equip the reception facility with a
piping system that, at its vessel side end, is fitted with a standard
discharge connection that meets the requirements of regulation 13 of
Annex I to MARPOL.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Overall advisory control of transfers
between oil tankers

- **[[36.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-36.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The authorized representative of an
  oil tanker of 150 gross tonnage or more must ensure that a transfer
  operation to which section 27.1 applies is under the overall advisory
  control of a person who meets the qualifications set out in section
  6.2.1.2 of the Manual on Oil Pollution, Section I -- Prevention,
  published by the IMO.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duties

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The person must carry out the duties set out in
  section 6.2.1.3 of the Manual.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2013-68, s. 7
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirements for transfer operations ---
vessels

**[[37]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-37 .sectionLabel}** The authorized
representative of a vessel that engages in a transfer operation must
ensure that it is supervised on board the vessel by a person who is
required to be part of the complement of the vessel

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a Canadian vessel, by paragraph
  207(3)(a), (b) or (c) or subparagraph 207(3)(d)(i) of the [Marine
  Personnel Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2007-115); and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a foreign vessel, by paragraph 241(a),
  subparagraph 241(b)(ii) or (iii) or paragraph 241(d) of the [Marine
  Personnel Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2007-115).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2013-68, s. 8
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-37-20120330.html "Link to previous version of section 37"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duties of supervisors of transfer
operations --- vessels

- **[[38]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-38 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  supervisor of a transfer operation on board a vessel must ensure that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the vessel is secured, having regard to the weather
    and the tidal and current conditions, and the mooring lines are
    tended so that the movement of the vessel does not damage the
    transfer conduit or its connections;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} transfer procedures are established with the
    concurrence of the supervisor of the transfer operation at the
    handling facility or on board the other vessel, as the case may be,
    with respect to

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the rates of flow and pressures for the
      transferred liquid,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the reduction of rates of flow and pressures,
      where required to avoid an overflow of the tanks,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the time required to stop the transfer
      operation under normal conditions,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the time required to shut down the transfer
      operation under emergency conditions, and

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} the communication signals for the transfer
      operation, including

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} stand by to start transfer,

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} start transfer,

      - [(C)]{.lawlabel} slow down transfer,

      - [(D)]{.lawlabel} stand by to stop transfer,

      - [(E)]{.lawlabel} stop transfer,

      - [(F)]{.lawlabel} emergency stop of transfer, and

      - [(G)]{.lawlabel} emergency shutdown of transfer;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the supervisor of the transfer operation at the
    handling facility or on board the other vessel, as the case may be,
    has reported readiness for the commencement of the transfer
    operation;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the person who is on duty on board the vessel in
    respect of the transfer operation is fully conversant with the
    communication signals, maintains watch over the vessel's tanks to
    ensure that they do not overflow, and maintains continuous
    communication with that person's counterpart at the handling
    facility or on board the other vessel, as the case may be;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the manifold valves and tank valves on the vessel
    are not closed until the relevant pumps are stopped, if the closing
    of the valves would cause dangerous over-pressurization of the
    pumping system;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the rate of flow is reduced when the tanks are
    being topped off;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the supervisor of the transfer operation at the
    handling facility or on board the other vessel, as the case may be,
    is given sufficient notice of the stopping of the transfer operation
    to permit them to take the necessary action to reduce the rate of
    flow or pressure in a safe and efficient manner;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the following measures are taken to prevent the
    discharge of oil:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} all cargo and bunker manifold connections that
      are not being used in the transfer operation are securely closed
      and fitted with blank flanges or other equivalent means of
      closure,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} all overboard discharge valves are securely
      closed and marked to indicate that they are not to be opened
      during the transfer operation, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} all scuppers are plugged;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a supply of peat moss or other absorbent material
    is readily available near every transfer conduit to facilitate the
    cleanup of any minor spillage of oil that may occur on the vessel or
    on the shore;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} all transfer conduits that are used in the transfer
    operation are supported to prevent the conduits and their
    connections from being subjected to any strain that might cause
    damage to them or cause the conduits to become disconnected;

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} all reasonable precautions are taken to avoid the
    discharge of oil; and

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} the vessel's STS operations Plan is implemented, if
    the vessel is of 150 gross tonnage or more and section 27.1 applies
    to the transfer operation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duties of supervisors of transfer
  operations --- facilities

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The supervisor of a transfer operation at a handling
  facility must ensure that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the supervisor of the transfer operation on board
    the vessel has reported readiness for the transfer operation to
    begin;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} continuous communication is maintained with the
    supervisor on board the vessel; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the manifold valves and the tank valves at the
    handling facility are not closed until the relevant pumps are
    stopped, if the closing of the valves would cause dangerous
    over-pressurization of the pumping system.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2013-68, s. 9
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-38-20120330.html "Link to previous version of section 38"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Emergency

**[[39]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-39 .sectionLabel}** In the event of an
emergency during a transfer operation, the master of a vessel and the
operator of a handling facility engaged in the operation must take all
necessary measures to rectify or minimize the emergency's effects.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notification of transfers between oil
tankers

- **[[39.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-39.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The master of an oil tanker of 150
  gross tonnage or more must ensure that the tanker does not engage in a
  transfer operation to which section 27.1 applies unless, at least 48
  hours before the transfer operation begins, the master gives notice in
  accordance with regulation 42.2 of Annex I to MARPOL to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the transfer operation is in waters under
    Canadian jurisdiction, a marine communications and traffic services
    officer; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the transfer operation is in the territorial sea
    or the exclusive economic zone of a foreign state that is a party to
    MARPOL, the appropriate official of the foreign state.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Information not available 48 hours
  before transfer

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), information that is specified
  in regulation 42.2 of Annex I to MARPOL and that is not, because of
  exceptional circumstances, available 48 hours before the transfer
  operation begins, does not need to be included with the notice. The
  master of an oil tanker that is planning to unload oil or an oily
  mixture must ensure that the tanker does not engage in the transfer
  operation unless the information that was not available is provided at
  the earliest opportunity to the marine communications and traffic
  services officer or the appropriate official, as the case may be.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Change in estimated arrival time

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the estimated time of arrival of an oil tanker at
  the location for the transfer operation changes by more than six
  hours, the master of the tanker must ensure that it does not engage in
  the transfer operation unless a revised estimated time of arrival is
  provided at the earliest opportunity to the marine communications and
  traffic services officer or the appropriate official, as the case may
  be.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2013-68, s. 10
:::

#### [SUBDIVISION 6]{.HLabel3}[Record-keeping]{.HTitleText3} {#h-790260 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Oil Record Books --- Part I

- **[[40]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-40 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every
  oil tanker of 150 gross tonnage or more, and every other vessel of
  400 gross tonnage or more that carries oil as cargo or as fuel, must
  keep on board an Oil Record Book, Part I (Machinery Space Operations)
  in the form set out in appendix III to Annex I to MARPOL.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Oil Record Books --- Part II

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every oil tanker of 150 gross tonnage or more must
  keep on board an Oil Record Book, Part II (Cargo/Ballast Operations)
  in the form set out in appendix III to Annex I to MARPOL.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Entries --- Part I --- officer in
  charge

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The officer in charge of a machinery space operation
  set out in regulation 17.2 of Annex I to MARPOL that takes place on a
  vessel referred to in subsection (1) must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} ensure that the operation is recorded without delay
    in the Oil Record Book, Part I (Machinery Space Operations); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} sign the recorded entry.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Entries --- Part I --- master

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The master of a vessel referred to in subsection (1)
  must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} ensure that

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the circumstances of and reasons for any
      discharge referred to in paragraph 5(a) or (b), or any other
      accidental or exceptional discharge to which paragraph 5(c) does
      not apply, of oil or an oily mixture are recorded without delay in
      the Oil Record Book, Part I (Machinery Space Operations), and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} any failure of the oil filtering equipment is
      recorded without delay in the Oil Record Book, Part I (Machinery
      Space Operations);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} ensure that each entry recorded in the Oil Record
    Book, Part I (Machinery Space Operations) is signed by the officer
    in charge of the operation; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} sign each page of the Oil Record Book, Part I
    (Machinery Space Operations) after the page is completed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Language --- Part I

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} An entry in the Oil Record Book, Part I must be
  written

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in English or French, in the case of a Canadian
    vessel or a Canadian pleasure craft; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in English, French or Spanish, in the case of a
    foreign vessel or a foreign pleasure craft.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Entries --- Part II --- officer in
  charge

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The officer in charge of a cargo/ballast operation
  set out in regulation 36.2 of Annex I to MARPOL that takes place on a
  vessel referred to in subsection (2) must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} ensure that the operation is recorded without delay
    in the Oil Record Book, Part II (Cargo/Ballast Operations); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} sign the recorded entry.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Entries --- Part II --- master

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The master of a vessel referred to in subsection (2)
  must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} ensure that

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the circumstances of and reasons for any
      discharge referred to in paragraph 5(a) or (b), or any other
      accidental or exceptional discharge, of oil or an oily mixture are
      recorded without delay in the Oil Record Book, Part II
      (Cargo/Ballast Operations), and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} any failure of the oil discharge monitoring and
      control system is recorded without delay in the Oil Record Book,
      Part II (Cargo/Ballast Operations);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} ensure that each entry recorded in the Oil Record
    Book, Part II (Cargo/Ballast Operations) is signed by the officer in
    charge of the operation; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} sign each page of the Oil Record Book, Part II
    (Cargo/Ballast Operations) after the page is completed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Language --- Part II

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} An entry in the Oil Record Book, Part II must be
  written

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in English or French, in the case of a Canadian
    vessel; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in English, French or Spanish, in the case of a
    foreign vessel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Three years

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} A vessel referred to in subsection (1) or (2) must
  keep the Oil Record Book, Part I and, if applicable, the Oil Record
  Book, Part II on board for three years after the day on which the last
  entry was made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Official log book

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} The Oil Record Book, Part I and, if applicable, the
  Oil Record Book, Part II may be part of the vessel's official log
  book.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reception facility receipts

- **[[41]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-41 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  master of a vessel must obtain from the owner or operator of a
  reception facility that receives oily residues from the vessel a
  receipt or certificate that sets out the type and amount of oily
  residues received and the date and time that they were received.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}One year

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The master must keep the receipt or certificate on
  board for one year after the day on which it was issued.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Recording device for bilge alarms

**[[42]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-42 .sectionLabel}** If an alarm on a vessel
is required under paragraph 12(1)(e) or (f) or subsection 30(2) to be
fitted with a recording device to meet the specifications of Part 2 of
the Annex to Resolution MEPC.107(49), the vessel must keep on board the
data that those specifications require to be recorded (the date, the
time, the alarm status of the alarm and the operating status of the oil
filtering equipment) for 18 months after the data is recorded.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}STS operations Plan --- Records

- **[[42.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-42.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The master of an oil tanker of 150
  gross tonnage or more that is required by section 27.1 to keep on
  board an STS operations Plan must ensure that the records required by
  the Plan are made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Three years

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The oil tanker must keep each record on board for
  three years after it is made.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2013-68, s. 11
:::

#### [SUBDIVISION 7]{.HLabel3}[Double Hulling for Oil Tankers]{.HTitleText3} {#h-790314 .Subheading}

##### [General]{.HTitleText4} {#h-790315 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

- **[[43]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-43 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} This
  section applies in respect of an oil tanker

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for which the building contract is placed after
    July 5, 1993;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that is constructed after January 5, 1994, in the
    absence of a building contract;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} that is first delivered after July 5, 1996; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} that undergoes a major conversion

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} for which the building contract is placed after
      July 5, 1993,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} for which the construction work begins after
      January 5, 1994, in the absence of a building contract, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} that is completed after July 5, 1996.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirements

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The authorized representative of an oil tanker must
  ensure that it

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} meets the design and construction requirements of
    regulations 19.3 and 19.4 of Annex I to MARPOL, or of
    regulation 19.6 of that Annex if applicable, unless the tanker is
    designed and constructed in accordance with the requirements of
    regulation 19.5 of that Annex; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is designed and constructed to facilitate the
    inspection and maintenance of wing and double bottom tanks or
    spaces.

##### [Phasing-in]{.HTitleText4} {#h-790330 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interpretation

- **[[44]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-44 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  this section, [[Category 2 oil tanker]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} and
  [[Category 3 oil tanker]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} have the same meaning as
  in regulations 20.3.2 and 20.3.3, respectively, of Annex I to MARPOL.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} This section applies in respect of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a foreign vessel that is an oil tanker referred to
    in regulation 20.1.1 of Annex I to MARPOL; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a Canadian vessel that is a Category 2 oil tanker
    or Category 3 oil tanker and that holds an International Oil
    Pollution Prevention Certificate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} This section does not apply in respect of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an oil tanker in respect of which section 43
    applies;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an oil tanker to which regulation 20.1.3 of Annex I
    to MARPOL applies and that, if applicable, meets the alternative
    requirements set out in that regulation;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an oil tanker that is entitled to fly the flag of
    the United States; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} an oil tanker that is engaged in the coasting trade
    as defined in subsection 2(1) of the [Coasting Trade
    Act](/eng/acts/C-33.3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirements

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (5) to (7), the authorized
  representative of an oil tanker must ensure that it meets the
  requirements of subsection 43(2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continued operation dependent on
  fittings

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A Category 2 oil tanker, or a Category 3 oil tanker,
  that is a Canadian vessel fitted with double hull spaces that are not
  used for the carriage of oil and extend over the entire cargo tank
  length but do not fulfil the conditions specified in regulation 20.1.3
  of Annex I to MARPOL, or fitted with only double bottoms or double
  sides that are not used for the carriage of oil and extend over the
  entire cargo tank length, may continue to operate if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} it was in service on July 1, 2001;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} its specifications set out in this subsection
    remain unchanged; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} its continued operation does not go beyond the day
    in the year 2015 that is the anniversary of the date of its delivery
    or the day on which it reaches 25 years of age, whichever is the
    earlier day.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continued operation dependent on
  Condition Assessment Scheme

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} A Category 2 oil tanker, or Category 3 oil tanker,
  that is a Canadian vessel may continue to operate if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the Board determines that the results of the
    Condition Assessment Scheme referred to in regulation 20.6 of
    Annex I to MARPOL indicate that the tanker is fit to continue to
    operate; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the tanker's continued operation does not go beyond
    the day in the year 2015 that is the anniversary of the date of its
    delivery or the day on which it reaches 25 years of age, whichever
    is the earlier day.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Foreign vessels operating under
  regulation 20.5 or 20.7

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (8), a Category 2 oil tanker,
  or a Category 3 oil tanker, that is a foreign vessel may operate if
  the government of the state whose flag the tanker is entitled to fly
  has allowed it to continue to operate under regulation 20.5 or 20.7 of
  Annex I to MARPOL.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibited to enter ports or offshore
  terminals

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} A Category 2 oil tanker, or a Category 3 oil tanker,
  that is a foreign vessel and is continuing to operate under
  regulation 20.5 of Annex I to MARPOL after the anniversary in the year
  2015 of the date of its delivery must not enter a port or an offshore
  terminal in waters under Canadian jurisdiction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Delivery and age

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this section,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the date of delivery of a tanker is the day on
    which it is first delivered; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the age of a tanker is determined from the day on
    which it is first delivered.

##### [Oil Tankers Carrying Heavy Grade Oil as Cargo]{.HTitleText4} {#h-790363 .Subheading}

Definition of [[heavy grade oil]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

- **[[45]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-45 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  this section, [[heavy grade oil]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same
  meaning as in regulation 21.2 of Annex I to MARPOL.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} This section applies in respect of an oil tanker of
  600 tonnes deadweight or more that is carrying heavy grade oil as
  cargo.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} This section does not apply in respect of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an oil tanker that is a Canadian vessel and engages
    only on voyages in waters under Canadian jurisdiction; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an oil tanker to which regulation 21.1.2 of Annex I
    to MARPOL applies and that, if applicable, meets the alternative
    requirements set out in that regulation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirements

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (5) to (8), the authorized
  representative of an oil tanker must ensure that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if it is of 5 000 tonnes deadweight or more, it
    meets the requirements of subsection 43(2); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if it is of 600 tonnes deadweight or more but less
    than 5 000 tonnes deadweight, it meets the requirements of
    subsection 43(2) or is fitted with

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} double bottom tanks or spaces that meet the
      requirements of regulation 19.6.1 of Annex I to MARPOL, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} wing tanks or spaces arranged in accordance with
      regulation 19.3.1 of Annex I to MARPOL that meet the requirement
      for distance, w, referred to in regulation 19.6.2 of Annex I to
      MARPOL.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continued operation dependent on
  fittings

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} An oil tanker that is a Canadian vessel of
  5 000 tonnes deadweight or more fitted with double hull spaces that
  are not used for the carriage of oil and extend over the entire cargo
  tank length but do not fulfil the conditions specified in
  regulation 21.1.2 of Annex I to MARPOL, or fitted with only double
  bottoms or double sides that are not used for the carriage of oil and
  extend over the entire cargo tank length, may continue to operate if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} it was in service on December 4, 2003;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} its specifications set out in this subsection
    remain unchanged; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} its continued operation does not go beyond the day
    in the year 2015 that is the anniversary of the date of its delivery
    or the day on which it reaches 25 years of age, whichever is the
    earlier day.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continued operation dependent on
  Condition Assessment Scheme

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} An oil tanker that is a Canadian vessel of
  5 000 tonnes deadweight or more that is carrying crude oil having a
  density at 15°C that is higher than 900 kg/m^3^ but lower than
  945 kg/m^3^ may continue to operate if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the Board determines, taking into consideration the
    tanker's size, age, operational area and structural conditions, that
    the results of the Condition Assessment Scheme referred to in
    regulation 20.6 of Annex I to MARPOL indicate that it is fit to
    continue to operate; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} its continued operation does not go beyond the day
    in the year 2015 that is the anniversary of the date of its delivery
    or the day on which it reaches 25 years of age, whichever is the
    earlier day.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continued operation --- heavy grade oil

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} An oil tanker that is a Canadian vessel of 600 tonnes
  deadweight or more but less than 5 000 tonnes deadweight and that does
  not meet the requirements of paragraph (4)(b) may continue to operate
  if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the Board determines, taking into consideration the
    tanker's size, age, operational area and structural conditions, that
    it is fit to continue to operate; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} its continued operation does not go beyond the day
    on which it reaches 25 years of age.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Foreign vessels

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} An oil tanker that is a foreign vessel may operate if
  the government of the state whose flag the tanker is entitled to fly
  has allowed the tanker to continue to operate under regulation 21.5,
  21.6.1 or 21.6.2 of Annex I to MARPOL.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Delivery and age

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this section,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the date of delivery of a tanker is the day on
    which it is first delivered; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the age of a tanker is determined from the day on
    which it is first delivered.

##### [Requirements for Other Oil Tankers]{.HTitleText4} {#h-790398 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

- **[[46]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-46 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} This
  section applies in respect of any oil tanker in respect of which
  sections 43 to 45 do not apply.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} This section does not apply in respect of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an oil tanker to which regulation 20.1.3 of Annex I
    to MARPOL applies and that, if applicable, meets the alternative
    requirements set out in that regulation; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an oil tanker that does not have mechanical means
    of propulsion and

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is of less than 2 000 gross tonnage,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} has no cargo tanks that exceed 200 m^3^
      capacity, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} engages only on voyages on

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} the Mackenzie River,

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} the waters contiguous to the Mackenzie River
        that are not within shipping safety control zone 12, or

      - [(C)]{.lawlabel} the rivers, streams or lakes that feed into the
        Mackenzie River.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Oil tankers of less than 5 000 gross
  tonnage

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The authorized representative of an oil tanker of
  less than 5 000 gross tonnage must ensure that it has a double hull or
  a double containment system determined by the Minister to be as
  effective as a double hull for the prevention of a discharge of oil.
  However, this requirement does not apply before January 1, 2015.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Oil tankers of 5 000 gross tonnage or
  more

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The authorized representative of an oil tanker of
  5 000 gross tonnage or more must ensure that it meets the requirements
  of subsection 43(2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (4) does not apply before January 1, 2015
  in respect of an oil tanker that has either double sides or double
  bottoms that are contiguous with cargo spaces and meet the applicable
  design and construction requirements of regulation 19.3 or 19.4, as
  the case may be, of Annex I to MARPOL, and

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is of less than 30 000 gross tonnage and less than
    30 years of age; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is of 30 000 gross tonnage or more and less than
    28 years of age.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Age

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (5), the age of an oil
  tanker is determined from the later of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the day on which it is first delivered, and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the day on which a major conversion is completed,
    if the conversion is completed before July 6, 1996.

##### [Oil Barges]{.HTitleText4} {#h-790424 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Height of double bottom

**[[47]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-47 .sectionLabel}** Despite
paragraph 43(2)(a), subsection 45(3), paragraph 45(4)(b) and
subsection 46(4), in the case of an oil tanker that is a Canadian vessel
of less than 5 000 tonnes deadweight that does not have mechanical means
of propulsion and engages only on voyages in waters under Canadian
jurisdiction within 40 nautical miles from the nearest land, the height
of its double bottom must in no location be less than the width
calculated for its wing tanks in accordance with the formula in
regulation 19.6.2 of Annex I to MARPOL.

#### [SUBDIVISION 8]{.HLabel3}[Exemptions and Equivalents]{.HTitleText3} {#h-790427 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Board

- **[[48]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-48 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Board may, in respect of Canadian vessels and Canadian pleasure craft,
  exercise the powers of the Administration conferred by regulations 3
  and 5 of Annex I to MARPOL.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Foreign governments

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In the case of a foreign vessel or a foreign pleasure
  craft, the requirements of this Division are subject to the exercise
  of the powers conferred by regulations 3 and 5 of Annex I to MARPOL by
  the government of the state whose flag the vessel is entitled to fly.

### [DIVISION 2]{.HLabel2}[Noxious Liquid Substances and Dangerous Chemicals]{.HTitleText2} {#h-790433 .Subheading}

#### [SUBDIVISION 1]{.HLabel3}[General]{.HTitleText3} {#h-790434 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limited application --- foreign vessels

**[[49]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-49 .sectionLabel}** This Division applies in
respect of foreign vessels in waters in the exclusive economic zone of
Canada only in respect of pollution.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Liquid substances

- **[[50]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-50 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  authorized representative and the master of a vessel must ensure that
  the vessel does not carry a liquid substance in bulk unless the
  substance

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is listed in chapter 17 or 18 of the IBC Code; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is provisionally assessed under regulation 6.3 of
    Annex II to MARPOL.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application to oil

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply in respect of oil.

#### [SUBDIVISION 2]{.HLabel3}[Construction and Equipment]{.HTitleText3} {#h-790444 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Plans and specifications

**[[51]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-51 .sectionLabel}** On application, the
Minister must approve plans and specifications with respect to a
Canadian vessel or a vessel that is recorded under the Act if the
matters described in the plans and specifications meet the applicable
requirements of this Subdivision.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}NLS tankers --- Annex II to MARPOL

- **[[52]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-52 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  authorized representative of an NLS tanker must ensure that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the requirements of regulation 12 of Annex II to
    MARPOL relating to the pumping, piping and unloading arrangements,
    the location and size of underwater discharge outlets, and the slop
    tanks or other arrangements are met; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the tanker uses ventilation procedures referred
    to in regulation 13.3 of Annex II to MARPOL to remove cargo residues
    from a tank, the tanker has ventilation equipment that can produce
    an air jet that reaches the tank bottom.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}IBC Code

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Any of the following vessels that were constructed on
  or after July 1, 1986, or that are Canadian vessels that were
  constructed before that date and first registered or listed in Canada
  after February 15, 1993, must meet the applicable design,
  construction, equipment and systems requirements of the IBC Code:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} vessels that carry a noxious liquid substance in
    bulk that is listed in chapter 17 of the IBC Code or is
    provisionally assessed under regulation 6.3 of Annex II to MARPOL as
    falling into Category X, Y or Z in the Pollution Category column of
    that chapter; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} chemical tankers that have means of
    self-propulsion.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}BCH Code

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Any of the following vessels that were constructed
  before July 1, 1986, other than Canadian vessels that were first
  registered or listed in Canada after February 15, 1993, must meet the
  applicable design, construction, equipment and systems requirements of
  the BCH Code:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} vessels that carry a noxious liquid substance in
    bulk that is listed in chapter 17 of the IBC Code or is
    provisionally assessed under regulation 6.3 of Annex II to MARPOL as
    falling into Category X, Y or Z in the Pollution Category column of
    that chapter; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} chemical tankers that have means of
    self-propulsion.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interpretation

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsections (2) and (3), a vessel
  converted to a chemical tanker, irrespective of the date of its
  construction, is considered to be a chemical tanker constructed on the
  date on which the conversion commenced unless the vessel

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} was constructed before July 1, 1986; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} was, when the conversion commenced, certified under
    the BCH Code to carry only those products identified by the Code as
    substances with pollution hazards only.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offshore support vessels

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Offshore support vessels may meet the applicable
  design, construction, equipment and systems requirements of
  Resolution A.673(16) instead of the requirements referred to in
  subsection (2) or (3).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Containers or enclosed deck areas --- NLS
tankers

- **[[53]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-53 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  authorized representative of an NLS tanker must ensure that each
  noxious liquid substance cargo manifold and each cargo transfer
  connection point on the tanker is fitted with a container or has an
  enclosed deck area that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} can retain noxious liquid substances that might
    leak or spill during transfer operations;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} has a means for the removal of the noxious liquid
    substances retained in it; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} does not adversely affect the stability of the
    tanker or the safety of its crew.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Volume

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the largest conduit serving a noxious liquid
  substance cargo manifold or a cargo transfer connection point on an
  NLS tanker has an inside diameter set out in column 1 of the table to
  subsection 15(2), the tanker's authorized representative must ensure
  that the container or enclosed deck area, under even-keel conditions,
  has the volume set out in column 2.

#### [SUBDIVISION 3]{.HLabel3}[Certificates, Endorsements and Inspections]{.HTitleText3} {#h-790474 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Issuance

- **[[54]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-54 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On
  application by the authorized representative of a Canadian vessel and
  subject to paragraphs 16(4)(b) to (d) of the Act, the Minister must
  issue

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a Canadian Noxious Liquid Substance Certificate to
    the vessel if the applicable requirements of this Division are met;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an International Pollution Prevention Certificate
    for the Carriage of Noxious Liquid Substances in Bulk to the vessel
    if the applicable requirements of Annex II to MARPOL are met;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an International Certificate of Fitness for the
    Carriage of Dangerous Chemicals in Bulk to the vessel if the
    applicable requirements of the IBC Code are met;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a Certificate of Fitness for the Carriage of
    Dangerous Chemicals in Bulk to the vessel if the applicable
    requirements of the BCH Code are met; or

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a Certificate of Fitness for an Offshore Support
    Vessel to the vessel if the applicable requirements of
    Resolution A.673(16) are met.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must, on the basis of the applicable
  requirements that are met, specify on the certificates issued under
  subsection (1) the particular substances or products that the vessel
  is certified to carry.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Endorsement of Canadian NLS certificates

- **[[55]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-55 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  authorized representative of a vessel that holds a Canadian Noxious
  Liquid Substance Certificate must ensure that the certificate is
  endorsed by the Minister, within three months before or after each
  anniversary date of the issuance of the certificate, to indicate that
  the requirements for the issuance of the certificate are met.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Endorsement of international NLS
  certificates

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The authorized representative of a vessel that holds
  an International Pollution Prevention Certificate for the Carriage of
  Noxious Liquid Substances in Bulk must ensure that the certificate is
  endorsed as required by regulations 8.1.3, 8.1.4 and 9.2 of Annex II
  to MARPOL.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Endorsement of IBC Code certificates of
  fitness

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The authorized representative of a vessel that holds
  an International Certificate of Fitness for the Carriage of Dangerous
  Chemicals in Bulk must ensure that the certificate is endorsed as
  required by chapter 1 of the IBC Code.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Endorsement of BCH Code certificates of
  fitness

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The authorized representative of a vessel that holds
  a Certificate of Fitness for the Carriage of Dangerous Chemicals in
  Bulk must ensure that the certificate is endorsed as required by
  chapter 1 of the BCH Code.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Endorsement of certificates of fitness
  for offshore support vessels

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The authorized representative of a vessel that holds
  a Certificate of Fitness for an Offshore Support Vessel must ensure
  that the certificate is endorsed as required by section 1.5.4 of
  Resolution A.673(16).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inspection

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} If the construction, arrangement, equipment,
  fittings, installations or systems of a vessel that holds a
  certificate issued under section 54 are changed as a result of an
  accident, the discovery of a defect, a repair or a major conversion
  that affects the requirements that were met when the certificate was
  issued, the authorized representative of the vessel must ensure that
  the Minister inspects the vessel as soon as feasible to ensure that
  the requirements continue to be met.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (6) does not apply in respect of minor
  repairs or the direct replacement of equipment or fittings that meet
  the requirements of the certificate.

#### [SUBDIVISION 4]{.HLabel3}[Shipboard Documents]{.HTitleText3} {#h-790500 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificates

- **[[56]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-56 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every
  NLS tanker and every chemical tanker must hold and keep on board

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a Canadian Noxious Liquid Substance Certificate, if
    the tanker is a Canadian vessel that engages only on voyages in
    waters under Canadian jurisdiction and carries noxious liquid
    substances in bulk but not dangerous chemicals in bulk;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an International Pollution Prevention Certificate
    for the Carriage of Noxious Liquid Substances in Bulk that is in the
    form set out in appendix 3 to Annex II to MARPOL, if the tanker

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is a Canadian vessel that does not engage only on
      voyages in waters under Canadian jurisdiction and carries noxious
      liquid substances in bulk but not dangerous chemicals in bulk, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} is entitled to fly the flag of a foreign state
      that is a party to MARPOL and carries noxious liquid substances in
      bulk but not dangerous chemicals in bulk;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a certificate of compliance certifying that the
    tanker meets the applicable requirements of Annex II to MARPOL, if
    the tanker is entitled to fly the flag of a state that is not a
    party to MARPOL and carries noxious liquid substances in bulk but
    not dangerous chemicals in bulk;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} an International Certificate of Fitness for the
    Carriage of Dangerous Chemicals in Bulk or a Certificate of Fitness
    for the Carriage of Dangerous Chemicals in Bulk that is in the form
    set out in the IBC Code or the BCH Code, as the case may be, if the
    tanker

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is a Canadian vessel that carries dangerous
      chemicals in bulk, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} is entitled to fly the flag of a foreign state
      that is a party to MARPOL and carries dangerous chemicals in bulk;
      or

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a certificate of compliance certifying that the
    tanker meets the applicable requirements of the IBC Code or the BCH
    Code, as the case may be, if the tanker is entitled to fly the flag
    of a state that is not a party to MARPOL and carries dangerous
    chemicals in bulk.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offshore support vessels

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An offshore support vessel may hold and keep on board
  a Certificate of Fitness for an Offshore Support Vessel instead of a
  Certificate required by subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Procedures and arrangements manual

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every NLS tanker must keep on board a procedures and
  arrangements manual that meets the requirements of regulation 14 of
  Annex II to MARPOL. In the case of a Canadian vessel, the expression
  "the requirements of this Annex" in regulation 14.2 of that Annex
  includes the requirements of section 67.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}IBC or BCH Codes

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Every chemical tanker must keep on board the IBC Code
  or the BCH Code, as the case may be.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Emergency plan

- **[[57]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-57
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), an NLS
  tanker of 150 gross tonnage or more must keep on board a shipboard
  marine pollution emergency plan for noxious liquid substances that
  meets the requirements of regulation 17.2 of Annex II to MARPOL.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If subsection 27(1) applies, the shipboard marine
  pollution emergency plan for noxious liquid substances may be combined
  with the shipboard oil pollution emergency plan, in which case the
  title of the plan must be the "shipboard marine pollution emergency
  plan".

#### [SUBDIVISION 5]{.HLabel3}[Notices]{.HTitleText3} {#h-790524 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Display

**[[58]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-58 .sectionLabel}** The master of an NLS
tanker or chemical tanker must ensure that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} permanent notices are displayed in conspicuous places
  on board, indicating the areas in which smoking and naked lights are
  prohibited;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} while the tanker is in a port, the following notices
  are displayed near every access to the tanker, as appropriate:

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} "NO NAKED LIGHTS / [PAS DE FLAMMES NUES]{.otherLang
    lang="fr"}",

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} "NO SMOKING / [DÉFENSE DE FUMER]{.otherLang
    lang="fr"}", and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} "NO UNAUTHORIZED PERSONS / [ACCÈS INTERDIT AUX
    PERSONNES NON AUTORISÉES]{.otherLang lang="fr"}"; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} while the tanker is in a port and the cargo being
  handled presents a health hazard, the notice "WARNING HAZARDOUS
  CHEMICALS / [ATTENTION PRODUITS CHIMIQUES DANGEREUX]{.otherLang
  lang="fr"}" is displayed near every access to the tanker.

#### [SUBDIVISION 6]{.HLabel3}[Control of Cargo Operations]{.HTitleText3} {#h-790533 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Operational requirements --- NLS tankers

**[[59]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-59 .sectionLabel}** The master of an NLS
tanker must ensure that the operational procedures with respect to cargo
handling, tank cleaning, slops handling and cargo tank ballasting and
deballasting are carried out in accordance with the tanker's procedures
and arrangements manual and with this Subdivision.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Operational requirements --- IBC Code

- **[[60]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-60 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  master of a chemical tanker that was constructed on or after July 1,
  1986, or that is a Canadian vessel that was constructed before that
  date and first registered or listed in Canada after February 15, 1993,
  must ensure that the operational requirements of the IBC Code are met.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Section 16.2.2 of the IBC Code

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The master of a chemical tanker referred to in
  subsection (1) must refuse any cargo mentioned in section 16.2.2 of
  the IBC Code if the analysis of the cargo has not been certified by
  its manufacturer or a marine chemist.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Operational requirements --- BCH Code

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The master of a chemical tanker that is not referred
  to in subsection (1) must ensure that the operational requirements of
  chapter V of the BCH Code are met.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offshore support vessels

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The master of an offshore support vessel that holds a
  Certificate of Fitness for an Offshore Support Vessel may ensure that
  the operational requirements of chapter 6 of Resolution A.673(16) are
  met instead of the requirements referred to in subsection (1) or (3).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Tank prewash operations

**[[61]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-61 .sectionLabel}** The tank prewash
operations referred to in sections 63 and 64 must be carried out

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} in accordance with the requirements of appendix 6 to
  Annex II to MARPOL; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} in such a way that the effluent resulting from the
  washing

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is transferred to a reception facility that can
    receive the effluent in an environmentally safe manner, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} is not discharged.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-60, s. 6](#1290833-1291185){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1290833-1291185.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-61-20120330.html "Link to previous version of section 61"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Stripping operations

- **[[62]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-62 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  cargo stripping operations that involve a Category Y noxious liquid
  substance are carried out at a handling facility, the operator of the
  facility must ensure that it can receive the cargo at an average flow
  rate of 6 m^3^ per hour without creating back pressure of more than
  100 kPa at the vessel's manifold.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Vessel's manifold

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The vessel's manifold must not be more than 3 m above
  the waterline at low mean tide.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cargo hoses and piping systems

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A cargo hose or piping system that contains a noxious
  liquid substance must not be drained back into the vessel after the
  completion of a cargo stripping operation.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Procedures --- Category X

- **[[63]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-63 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A tank
  from which a Category X noxious liquid substance has been unloaded
  must undergo a tank prewash operation before the vessel leaves the
  port of unloading unless the vessel has been granted an exemption from
  a tank prewash operation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exemption

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On request by the master of a vessel, a marine safety
  inspector may grant an exemption from a tank prewash operation if the
  inspector is satisfied that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the unloaded tank will be reloaded with the same
    substance or another substance compatible with the previous one and
    will not be washed or ballasted prior to loading;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the unloaded tank will not be washed or ballasted
    at sea and the vessel's master has given written notice to the
    Department of Transport Marine Safety Office nearest to the vessel
    that the tank will undergo a tank prewash operation at another port
    and that this other port has confirmed in writing that it has a
    reception facility that is available and adequate for such a
    purpose; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the cargo residues will be removed from the tank by
    a ventilation procedure.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Transfer of effl‡uent

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (4), the tank prewash operation
  must be carried out by washing the tank and transferring the effluent
  to a reception facility that can receive the effluent in an
  environmentally safe manner until the concentration of the substance
  in the effluent has fallen to 0.1% by weight, and then by continuing
  to transfer the remaining effluent to the facility until the tank is
  empty.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}If not feasible

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If it is not feasible to measure the concentration of
  the substance to ensure that it falls within the limit specified in
  subsection (3) without causing undue delay to the vessel, the tank
  prewash operation must be carried out by washing the tank and
  transferring the effluent to a reception facility in accordance with
  subparagraph 61(b)(i).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Marine safety inspector

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The tank prewash operation must be carried out in the
  presence of a marine safety inspector who endorses the appropriate
  entries in the vessel's Cargo Record Book required by subsection
  79(1).

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-60, s. 7](#1290835-1291201){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1290835-1291201.html"
  role="button"}
:::
