[Previous
Version](section-63-20120330.html "Link to previous version of section 63"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interpretation

- **[[64]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-64 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  following definitions apply in this section.

  [[high-viscosity substance]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[high-viscosity substance]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a noxious
      liquid substance in Category X or Y with a viscosity equal to or
      greater than 50 mPa·s at the unloading temperature. ([substance à
      viscosité élevée]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[solidifying substance]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[solidifying substance]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a noxious
      liquid substance that at the time of unloading is

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} at a temperature of less than 5°C above its
        melting point, in the case of a substance with a melting point
        below 15°C; or

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} at a temperature of less than 10°C above its
        melting point, in the case of a substance with a melting point
        of 15°C or higher. ([substance qui se
        solidifie]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Procedures --- Categories Y and Z

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A tank from which a Category Y or Z noxious liquid
  substance has been unloaded must undergo a tank prewash operation
  before the vessel leaves the port of unloading if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the unloaded substance is a Category Y noxious
    liquid substance that is a high-viscosity substance or a solidifying
    substance; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the unloading operation is not carried out in
    accordance with the vessel's procedures and arrangements manual.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (2) does not apply if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the unloaded tank will be reloaded with the same
    substance or another substance compatible with the previous one and
    will not be washed or ballasted prior to loading;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the unloaded tank will not be washed or ballasted
    at sea and the vessel's master has given written notice to the
    Department of Transport Marine Safety Office nearest to the vessel
    that the tank will undergo a tank prewash operation at another port
    and that this other port has confirmed in writing that it has a
    reception facility that is available and adequate for such a
    purpose; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the cargo residues will be removed from the tank by
    a ventilation procedure.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-60, s. 8](#1290837-1291209){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1290837-1291209.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-64-20120330.html "Link to previous version of section 64"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ventilation procedures

**[[65]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-65 .sectionLabel}** A ventilation procedure
must not be used to remove cargo residues for the purposes of paragraph
63(2)(c) or 64(3)(c) unless

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the noxious liquid substance that was unloaded has a
  vapour pressure greater than 5 kPa at 20°C; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the ventilation is carried out in accordance with
  sections 2 and 4 of appendix 7 to Annex II to MARPOL.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-60, s. 9](#1290842-1291212){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1290842-1291212.html"
  role="button"}
:::

Version](section-65-20120330.html "Link to previous version of section 65"){.PITLink}

#### [SUBDIVISION 7]{.HLabel3}[Discharges of Noxious Liquid Substances]{.HTitleText3} {#h-790590 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

**[[66]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66 .sectionLabel}** This subdivision does not
apply in respect of vessels in a shipping safety control zone or of
Canadian vessels in an area in respect of which subsection 7(2) applies.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2017-286, s. 32
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-66-20120330.html "Link to previous version of section 66"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition

- **[[67]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-67 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person or vessel must not discharge a noxious liquid substance carried
  in bulk except

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in accordance with sections 68 to 71, in the case
    of a discharge from an NLS tanker in Section II waters or from a
    Canadian vessel that is an NLS tanker in waters that are not waters
    under Canadian jurisdiction; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the circumstances set out in section 5 that
    apply in respect of the discharge.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offshore support vessels

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Paragraph (1)(a) does not apply in respect of a
  vessel that holds a Certificate of Fitness for an Offshore Support
  Vessel.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authorized discharge --- Category X

**[[68]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-68 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
section 67, ballast water that contains a Category X noxious liquid
substance only because the ballast water was introduced into a tank that
last contained that substance may be discharged if

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the tank underwent a tank prewash operation in
  accordance with subsection 63(3);

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the vessel is en route at a speed of at least
  7 knots, if it is self-propelled, or at least 4 knots, if it is not
  self-propelled; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the discharge is made

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in accordance with the vessel's procedures and
    arrangements manual,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} below the waterline through an underwater
    discharge outlet at a rate that does not exceed the maximum rate for
    which the underwater discharge outlet was designed,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} at a distance of at least 12 nautical miles from
    the nearest land, and

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} into waters whose depth is at least 25 m.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-60, s. 10](#1290844-1291215){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1290844-1291215.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-68-20120330.html "Link to previous version of section 68"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authorized discharge --- Category Y

**[[69]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-69 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
section 67, a Category Y noxious liquid substance may be discharged if

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the vessel is en route at a speed of at least
  7 knots, if it is self-propelled, or at least 4 knots, if it is not
  self-propelled; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the discharge is made

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in accordance with the vessel's procedures and
    arrangements manual,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} below the waterline through an underwater
    discharge outlet at a rate that does not exceed the maximum rate for
    which the underwater discharge outlet was designed,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} at a distance of at least 12 nautical miles from
    the nearest land, and

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} into waters whose depth is at least 25 m.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authorized discharge --- Category Z

**[[70]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-70 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
section 67, a Category Z noxious liquid substance may be discharged if

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the vessel is en route at a speed of at least
  7 knots, if it is self-propelled, or at least 4 knots, if it is not
  self-propelled; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the discharge is made

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in accordance with the vessel's procedures and
    arrangements manual,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a vessel constructed after
    December 31, 2006, below the waterline through an underwater
    discharge outlet at a rate that does not exceed the maximum rate for
    which the underwater discharge outlet was designed;

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} at a distance of at least 12 nautical miles from
    the nearest land, and

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} into waters whose depth is at least 25 m.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authorized discharge --- ballast water

- **[[71]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-71 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purposes of section 67, a Category Y or Z noxious liquid substance
  in ballast water that is introduced into a cargo tank that last
  carried the substance and has been washed to such an extent that the
  ballast water contains less than 1 ppm of the noxious liquid substance
  may be discharged without regard to the discharge rate, vessel speed
  and discharge outlet location if the discharge is made at a distance
  of at least 12 nautical miles from the nearest land and into waters
  whose depth is at least 25 m.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}NLS tankers constructed before July 1,
  1994

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In the case of an NLS tanker constructed before
  July 1, 1994, the ballast water is considered to contain less than
  1 ppm of the noxious liquid substance previously carried if the cargo
  tank

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is washed in accordance with the requirements of
    Part A of appendix 6 to Annex II to MARPOL and subsequently washed
    with a complete cycle of the cleaning machine; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is washed with a water quantity not less than that
    required by paragraph 20 of Part B of appendix 6 to Annex II to
    MARPOL, using a k factor of 1.0 in the formula set out in that
    paragraph.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other vessels

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In the case of an NLS tanker other than one referred
  to in subsection (2), the ballast water is considered to contain less
  than 1 ppm of the noxious liquid substance previously carried if the
  cargo tank is washed in accordance with the requirements of Part B of
  appendix 6 to Annex II to MARPOL.

#### [SUBDIVISION 8]{.HLabel3}[Transfer Operations]{.HTitleText3} {#h-790636 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

**[[72]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-72 .sectionLabel}** This Subdivision applies
in respect of vessels only when they are in waters under Canadian
jurisdiction or, if a transfer operation to which this Subdivision
applies does not involve a noxious liquid substance, only when they are
in Canadian waters.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Communications

**[[73]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-73 .sectionLabel}** If a vessel or a handling
facility engages in a transfer operation, the vessel's master and the
operator of the facility must, before and during the transfer operation,
have the means for two-way voice communication on a continuing basis
that enables the supervisor on board the vessel and the supervisor at
the facility or on board the other vessel

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} to communicate immediately as the need arises; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} to direct the immediate shutdown of the transfer
  operation in case of an emergency.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Lighting

- **[[74]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-74 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  vessel or a handling facility engages in a transfer operation between
  sunset and sunrise, the vessel's master and the operator of the
  facility must ensure that illumination is provided that has

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a lighting intensity of not less than 54 lx at each
    transfer connection point of the vessel or facility; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a lighting intensity of not less than 11 lx at each
    transfer operation work area around each transfer connection point
    of the vessel or facility.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Measurement

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (1), lighting
  intensity is to be measured on a horizontal plane 1 m above the
  walking surface of the facility or the working deck of the vessel, as
  applicable.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Transfer conduits

- **[[75]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-75 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person must not use a transfer conduit in a transfer operation unless
  the conduit

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} has a bursting pressure of not less than five times
    its maximum design pressure;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is clearly marked with its maximum design pressure;
    and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} has successfully passed, during the year before its
    use, a hydrostatic test to a pressure equal to one and one-half
    times its maximum design pressure.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Test certificates

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a transfer conduit used in a transfer operation is
  part of a vessel's equipment, the vessel's master must keep on board
  the test certificate for the hydrostatic test.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Manufacturer's specifications

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The owner of a transfer conduit that is used in a
  transfer operation must ensure that the conduit is used, maintained,
  tested and replaced in accordance with the manufacturer's
  specifications.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Leaks

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If a transfer conduit or a connection leaks during a
  transfer operation, the supervisor on board the vessel and the
  supervisor at the handling facility or on board the other vessel must,
  as soon as feasible, slow down or stop the operation to remove the
  pressure from the conduit or connection.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirements for transfer operations ---
vessels

**[[76]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-76 .sectionLabel}** The authorized
representative of a vessel that engages in a transfer operation must
ensure that it is supervised on board the vessel by a person who is
required to be part of the complement of the vessel

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a Canadian vessel, by paragraph
  207(3)(a), (b) or (c) or subparagraph 207(3)(d)(i) of the [Marine
  Personnel Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2007-115); and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a foreign vessel, by paragraph 241(a),
  subparagraph 241(b)(ii) or (iii) or paragraph 241(d) of the [Marine
  Personnel Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2007-115).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2013-68, s. 12
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-76-20120330.html "Link to previous version of section 76"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duties of supervisors of transfer
operations --- vessels

- **[[77]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  supervisor of a transfer operation on board a vessel must ensure that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the vessel is secured, having regard to the weather
    and the tidal and current conditions, and the mooring lines are
    tended so that the movement of the vessel does not damage the
    transfer conduit or its connections;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} transfer procedures are established with the
    concurrence of the supervisor of the transfer operation at the
    handling facility or on board the other vessel, as the case may be,
    with respect to

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the rates of flow and pressures for the
      transferred liquid,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the reduction of rates of flow and pressures
      where required to avoid an overflow of the tanks,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the time required to stop the transfer
      operation under normal conditions,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the time required to shut down the transfer
      operation under emergency conditions, and

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} the communication signals for the transfer
      operation, including

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} stand by to start transfer,

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} start transfer,

      - [(C)]{.lawlabel} slow down transfer,

      - [(D)]{.lawlabel} stand by to stop transfer,

      - [(E)]{.lawlabel} stop transfer,

      - [(F)]{.lawlabel} emergency stop of transfer, and

      - [(G)]{.lawlabel} emergency shutdown of transfer;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the supervisor of the transfer operation at the
    handling facility or on board the other vessel, as the case may be,
    has reported readiness for the commencement of the transfer
    operation;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the person who is on duty on board the vessel in
    respect of the transfer operation is fully conversant with the
    communication signals, maintains watch over the vessel's tanks to
    ensure that they do not overflow, and maintains continuous
    communication with that person's counterpart at the handling
    facility or on board the other vessel, as the case may be;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the manifold valves and tank valves on the vessel
    are not closed until the relevant pumps are stopped, if the closing
    of the valves would cause dangerous over-pressurization of the
    pumping system;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the rate of flow is reduced when the tanks are
    being topped off;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the supervisor of the transfer operation at the
    handling facility or on board the other vessel, as the case may be,
    is given sufficient notice of the stopping of the transfer operation
    to permit them to take the necessary action to reduce the rate of
    flow or pressure in a safe and efficient manner;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the following measures are taken to prevent the
    discharge of a noxious liquid substance or dangerous chemical:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} all cargo manifold connections that are not being
      used in the transfer operation are securely closed and fitted with
      blank flanges or other equivalent means of closure,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} all overboard discharge valves are securely
      closed and marked to indicate that they are not to be opened
      during the transfer operation, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} all scuppers are plugged;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a supply of absorbent material is readily available
    near every transfer conduit to facilitate the clean-up of any minor
    spillage of noxious liquid substances or dangerous chemicals that
    may occur on the vessel or on the shore;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} all transfer conduits that are used in the transfer
    operation are supported to prevent the conduits and their
    connections from being subjected to any strain that might cause
    damage to them or cause the conduits to become disconnected;

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} all systems, equipment, personnel and information
    necessary for the safety of the transfer are in readiness before the
    transfer operation begins;

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} towing-off wires are positioned fore and aft and
    are ready for use without adjustment if it is necessary to tow the
    vessel away;

  - [(m)]{.lawlabel} the transfer of a flammable cargo and the
    gas-freeing that follows the unloading of a flammable cargo is
    stopped when an electrical storm is in the immediate vicinity of the
    vessel;

  - [(n)]{.lawlabel} no work is carried out in the cargo tank area
    without the authorization of the vessel's master;

  - [(o)]{.lawlabel} the valves in the vent system are checked for the
    correct setting and the flame arresters are examined for cleanliness
    and proper installation;

  - [(p)]{.lawlabel} articulated loading booms, if used, are checked for
    undue strain;

  - [(q)]{.lawlabel} the pump room ventilation is running and all
    precautions for that area are observed;

  - [(r)]{.lawlabel} a tank that is required to be kept in an inert
    state (a state in which the oxygen content of the tank must be below
    a specified level), and to maintain a small positive pressure at all
    times, has a supply of inert gas available to maintain its inert
    state during the transfer operation;

  - [(s)]{.lawlabel} during loading,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the tank concerned is free of flammable or toxic
      vapours or residues,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the free end of the loading hose is securely
      lashed to the inside of the tank to prevent movement,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} all flanges and gaskets are suitable for the
      purpose, and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} all tank openings, other than those that are in
      use, are closed; and

  - [(t)]{.lawlabel} all reasonable precautions are taken to avoid the
    discharge of a noxious liquid substance or dangerous chemical.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duties of supervisors of transfer
  operations --- facilities

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The supervisor of a transfer operation at a handling
  facility must ensure that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the supervisor of the transfer operation on board
    the vessel has reported readiness for the transfer operation to
    begin;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} continuous communication is maintained with the
    supervisor on board the vessel; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the manifold valves and the tank valves at the
    handling facility are not closed until the relevant pumps are
    stopped, if the closing of the valves would cause dangerous
    over-pressurization of the pumping system.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Emergencies

**[[78]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-78 .sectionLabel}** In the event of an
emergency during a transfer operation, the master of a vessel and the
operator of a handling facility engaged in the operation must take all
necessary measures to rectify or minimize the emergency's effects.

#### [SUBDIVISION 9]{.HLabel3}[Record-keeping]{.HTitleText3} {#h-790717 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cargo Record Books for NLS tankers

- **[[79]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-79 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every
  NLS tanker must keep on board a Cargo Record Book in the form set out
  in appendix 2 to Annex II to MARPOL.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Entries --- officer in charge

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The officer in charge of an operation set out in
  appendix 2 to Annex II to MARPOL that takes place on an NLS tanker
  must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} ensure that the operation is recorded without delay
    in the Cargo Record Book; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} sign the recorded entry.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Entries --- master

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The vessel's master must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} ensure that the circumstances of and reasons for
    any discharge referred to in paragraph 5(a) or (b), or any other
    accidental or exceptional discharge, of a noxious liquid substance
    carried in bulk are recorded without delay in the Cargo Record Book;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} ensure that each entry recorded in the Cargo Record
    Book is signed by the officer in charge of the operation; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} sign each page of the Cargo Record Book after the
    page is completed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Language

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An entry in the Cargo Record Book must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a Canadian vessel, be written in
    English or French; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a foreign vessel, be written in
    English, French or Spanish.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Three years

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The vessel must keep the Cargo Record Book on board
  for three years after the day on which the last entry was made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Official log book

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The Cargo Record Book may be part of the vessel's
  official log book.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reception facility receipts

- **[[80]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-80 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  master of a vessel must obtain from the owner or operator of a
  reception facility that receives noxious liquid substance residues or
  mixtures from the vessel a receipt or certificate that sets out the
  type and amount of noxious liquid substance residues or mixtures
  received and the date and time that they were received.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}One year

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The master must keep the receipt or certificate on
  board for one year after the day on which it was issued.

#### [SUBDIVISION 10]{.HLabel3}[Exemptions and Equivalents]{.HTitleText3} {#h-790743 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Board

- **[[81]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-81 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Board may, in respect of Canadian vessels, exercise the powers of the
  Administration conferred by regulations 4 and 5 of Annex II to MARPOL.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Foreign governments

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In the case of a foreign vessel, the requirements of
  this Division are subject to the exercise of the powers conferred by
  regulations 4 and 5 of Annex II to MARPOL, section 1.4 of the IBC
  Code, section 1.5 of the BCH Code and section 1.4 of
  Resolution A.673(16) by the government of the state whose flag the
  vessel is entitled to fly.

### [DIVISION 3]{.HLabel2}[Marine Pollutants]{.HTitleText2} {#h-790749 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Discharge prohibited

- **[[82]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-82 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person or vessel must not discharge a marine pollutant that is not
  carried in bulk except in accordance with subsection (2) or in the
  circumstances set out in section 5 that apply in respect of the
  discharge.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authorized discharge

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (1), a leakage of a
  marine pollutant that is not carried in bulk and is not kept as ships'
  stores may be discharged if any of the spillage schedules to The EmS
  Guide: Emergency Response Procedures for Ships Carrying Dangerous
  Goods, published by the IMO, sets out procedures in respect of the
  leakage and those procedures are followed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Jettisoning

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A person or vessel must not jettison a marine
  pollutant that is carried in packaged form unless it is necessary to
  do so for the purpose of saving lives, securing the safety of a vessel
  or preventing the immediate loss of a vessel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limited application --- pleasure craft

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} This section applies in respect of pleasure craft
  that are not Canadian vessels only when they are in Canadian waters.

### [DIVISION 4]{.HLabel2}[Sewage]{.HTitleText2} {#h-790759 .Subheading}

#### [SUBDIVISION 1]{.HLabel3}[General]{.HTitleText3} {#h-790760 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interpretation

**[[83]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-83 .sectionLabel}** The following definitions
apply in this Division.

[[designated sewage area]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[designated sewage area]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an area set out
    in Schedule 2. ([zone désignée pour les eaux usées]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[holding tank]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[holding tank]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a tank that is used
    solely for the collection and storage of sewage or sewage sludge and
    includes a tank that is an integral part of a toilet. ([citerne de
    retenue]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[inland waters of Canada]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[inland waters of Canada]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means all the
    rivers, lakes and other navigable fresh waters within Canada, and
    includes the St. Lawrence River as far seaward as a straight line
    drawn from Pointe-au-Père to Orient Point. ([eaux internes du
    Canada]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[marine sanitation device]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[marine sanitation device]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}[\[Repealed,
    SOR/2013-68, s. 13\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2013-68, s. 13
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-83-20120330.html "Link to previous version of section 83"){.PITLink}
:::

Definition of [[existing vessel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

- **[[84]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-84 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  this section, [[existing vessel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a vessel

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for which the building contract was placed before
    May 3, 2007;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the absence of a building contract, the keel of
    which was laid or that was at a similar stage of construction before
    May 3, 2007; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the delivery of which was before May 3, 2010.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} This Division does not apply before May 3, 2012 in
  respect of an existing vessel that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is of less than 400 gross tonnage, is not certified
    to carry more than 15 persons and is engaged on an international
    voyage; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is not engaged on an international voyage.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Discharge requirements

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (2),

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} Subdivision 2, section 95 and paragraph 96(1)(a)
    apply on the coming into force of this section in respect of all
    vessels that are in the Great Lakes, their connecting or tributary
    waters, or the St. Lawrence River as far east as the lower exit of
    the St. Lambert Lock at Montréal, Quebec; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} Subdivision 2, section 95 and paragraph 96(1)(b)
    apply on the coming into force of this section in respect of all
    vessels that are in a designated sewage area.

#### [SUBDIVISION 2]{.HLabel3}[Equipment]{.HTitleText3} {#h-790783 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Plans and specifications

**[[85]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-85 .sectionLabel}** On application, the
Minister must approve plans and specifications with respect to a
Canadian vessel or a vessel that is recorded under the Act if the
matters described in the plans and specifications meet the applicable
requirements of this Subdivision.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Vessels with toilet facilities

- **[[86]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-86
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (3) and (4),
  the authorized representative of a vessel in Section I waters or
  Section II waters that has a toilet must ensure that the vessel is
  fitted with a holding tank or a marine sanitation device that meets
  the requirements of section 88 or 90, as the case may be.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Temporary storage of sewage

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The vessel's authorized representative must ensure
  that it is fitted with facilities for the temporary storage of sewage
  if the vessel

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is fitted with a marine sanitation device that
    meets the requirements of paragraph 90(1)(d) only; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is in a designated sewage area and is fitted with a
    marine sanitation device that does not meet the requirements of
    paragraph 90(1)(b).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of meeting the requirement of
  subsection (1), a Canadian vessel or Canadian pleasure craft must not
  be fitted with a marine sanitation device referred to in
  paragraph 90(1)(d).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A vessel referred to in subsection (1) that is of
  less than 15 gross tonnage, is not certified to carry more than
  15 persons and is not operating in the inland waters of Canada or
  designated sewage areas may be fitted with facilities for the
  temporary storage of sewage instead of meeting the requirements of
  subsection (1) if it is not feasible to meet those requirements and
  the vessel has measures in place to ensure that no discharge is made
  otherwise than in accordance with section 96.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Securing toilets

**[[87]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-87 .sectionLabel}** The authorized
representative of a vessel must ensure that any toilet fitted on the
vessel is secured in a manner that ensures its safe operation in any
environmental conditions likely to be encountered.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Holding tanks

**[[88]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-88 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
subsection 86(1), a holding tank must

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} be constructed so that it does not compromise the
  integrity of the hull;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} be constructed of structurally sound material that
  prevents the tank contents from leaking;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} be constructed so that the potable water system and
  other systems cannot become contaminated;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} be resistant to corrosion by sewage;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} have an adequate volume for the amount of sewage that
  could be reasonably expected to be produced on a voyage in waters
  where the discharge of sewage is not authorized by section 96;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} be provided with a discharge connection and piping
  system for the removal of the tank contents at a reception facility;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} be designed so that the level of sewage in the tank
  can be determined without the tank being opened and without contacting
  or removing any of the tank contents, or be equipped with a device
  that allows the determination to be made;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a vessel, other than a pleasure craft,
  that operates solely on the Great Lakes and their connecting waters,
  be equipped with an alarm that indicates when the tank is 75% full by
  volume; and

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} be equipped with a ventilation device that

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} has its outlet located on the exterior of the
    vessel and in a safe location away from ignition sources and areas
    usually occupied by people,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} prevents the build-up within the tank of pressure
    that could cause damage to the tank,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} is designed to minimize clogging by the contents
    of the tank or by climatic conditions such as snow or ice,

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} is constructed of material that cannot be corroded
    by sewage, and

  - [(v)]{.lawlabel} has a flame screen of non-corrosive material fitted
    to the vent outlet.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Transfer conduits

- **[[89]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-89 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person must not use a transfer conduit for the purpose of removing
  sewage or sewage sludge from a holding tank or a temporary means of
  storage on a vessel to a reception facility unless it is used,
  maintained and secured in a manner that minimizes risk to the marine
  environment from a discharge of sewage or sewage sludge.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Leaks

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a transfer conduit or a connection leaks during
  the removal of sewage or sewage sludge from a holding tank or a
  temporary means of storage on a vessel to a reception facility, the
  vessel's master must, as soon as feasible, ensure that the removal
  operation is slowed down or stopped to remove the pressure from the
  conduit or connection.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Marine sanitation devices

- **[[90]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-90 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purposes of subsection 86(1), a marine sanitation device must meet

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the requirements of regulation 9.1.1 of Annex IV to
    MARPOL for a sewage treatment plant;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} requirements substantially similar to the
    requirements referred to in paragraph (a) except that the standards
    referred to in regulation 9.1.1 include the effluent standard set
    out in paragraph 96(1)(b);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the design, construction and testing requirements
    of Title 33, Part 159, Subpart C of the Code of Federal Regulations
    of the United States for a Type II marine sanitation device; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the requirements of regulation 9.1.2 of Annex IV to
    MARPOL for a sewage comminuting and disinfecting system.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Grandfathering

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), a marine sanitation device
  that was approved as an approved device under the [Great Lakes Sewage
  Pollution Prevention Regulations](/eng/regulations/C.R.C.,_c._1429)
  and continues to meet the requirements of those Regulations as they
  read on May 2, 2007 may continue to be used as a marine sanitation
  device.

#### [SUBDIVISION 3]{.HLabel3}[Certificates and Inspections]{.HTitleText3} {#h-790829 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Issuance of International Sewage
Pollution Prevention Certificates

**[[91]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-91 .sectionLabel}** On application by the
authorized representative of a Canadian vessel or a Canadian pleasure
craft and subject to paragraphs 16(4)(b) to (d) of the Act, the Minister
must issue an International Sewage Pollution Prevention Certificate to
the vessel if the applicable requirements of Annex IV to MARPOL are met.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inspection

- **[[92]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-92 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If the
  construction, arrangement, equipment, fittings, installations or
  systems of a vessel that holds a certificate issued under section 91
  are changed as a result of an accident, the discovery of a defect, a
  repair or a major conversion that affects the requirements that were
  met when the certificate was issued, the authorized representative of
  the vessel must ensure that the Minister inspects the vessel as soon
  as feasible to ensure that the requirements continue to be met.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply in respect of minor
  repairs or the direct replacement of equipment or fittings that meet
  the requirements of the certificate.

#### [SUBDIVISION 4]{.HLabel3}[Shipboard Documents]{.HTitleText3} {#h-790837 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificates

- **[[93]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-93 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every
  vessel of 400 gross tonnage or more and every vessel that is certified
  to carry more than 15 persons must hold and keep on board

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an International Sewage Pollution Prevention
    Certificate in the form set out in the appendix to Annex IV to
    MARPOL, if the vessel

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is a Canadian vessel or a Canadian pleasure craft
      and does not engage only on voyages in waters under Canadian
      jurisdiction, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} is entitled to fly the flag of a foreign state
      that is a party to Annex IV to MARPOL; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a certificate of compliance certifying that the
    vessel meets the applicable requirements of Annex IV to MARPOL, if
    the vessel is entitled to fly the flag of a state that is not a
    party to Annex IV to MARPOL.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificates of type approval

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every vessel that is fitted with a marine sanitation
  device in order to meet the requirements of subsection 86(1) must keep
  on board a certificate of type approval

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a device referred to in
    subsection 90(1), certifying that the device meets the applicable
    requirements referred to in that subsection; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a device referred to in
    subsection 90(2), certifying that the device was approved as an
    approved device under the [Great Lakes Sewage Pollution Prevention
    Regulations](/eng/regulations/C.R.C.,_c._1429) and bearing the
    approval number.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Operation and maintenance manual

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every vessel that is fitted with a marine sanitation
  device in order to meet the requirements of subsection 86(1) and is of
  400 gross tonnage or more or certified to carry more than 15 persons
  must keep on board a manual that sets out the operational and
  maintenance procedures for the device.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sewage effluent records

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Every vessel must keep on board for 12 months an
  English or French version of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a record of the results of any tests required by
    subsection 97(2); or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the records required by subsection 97(4).

#### [SUBDIVISION 5]{.HLabel3}[Discharges of Sewage or Sewage Sludge]{.HTitleText3} {#h-790855 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

**[[94]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-94 .sectionLabel}** This Subdivision does not
apply in respect of

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} vessels in a shipping safety control zone; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} pleasure craft that are not Canadian vessels and that
  are in waters in the exclusive economic zone of Canada.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition

**[[95]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-95 .sectionLabel}** A person or vessel must
not discharge sewage or sewage sludge except in accordance with
section 96 or in the circumstances set out in section 5 that apply in
respect of the discharge.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authorized discharge

- **[[96]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-96 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purposes of section 95, sewage may be discharged if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a vessel in an area other than a
    designated sewage area, the discharge is passed through a marine
    sanitation device and the effluent has a fecal coliform count that
    is equal to or less than 250/100 mL;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a vessel in a designated sewage
    area, the discharge is passed through a marine sanitation device and
    the effluent has a fecal coliform count that is equal to or less
    than 14/100 mL;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a vessel that is in Section I waters
    or Section II waters, but not in the inland waters of Canada or a
    designated sewage area, and that is of 400 gross tonnage or more or
    is certified to carry more than 15 persons,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the discharge is made at a distance of at least
      12 nautical miles from shore and, if it is made from a holding
      tank or from facilities for the temporary storage of sewage, at a
      moderate rate while the vessel is en route at a speed of at least
      4 knots, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the sewage is comminuted and disinfected using a
      marine sanitation device and the discharge is made at a distance
      of at least 3 nautical miles from shore;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a Canadian vessel that is in waters
    that are not waters under Canadian jurisdiction, other than the
    Antarctic area, and that is of 400 gross tonnage or more or is
    certified to carry more than 15 persons,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the discharge is made at a distance of at least
      12 nautical miles from the nearest land and, if it is made from a
      holding tank or from facilities for the temporary storage of
      sewage, at a moderate rate while the vessel is en route at a speed
      of at least 4 knots, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the sewage is comminuted and disinfected using a
      marine sanitation device and the discharge is made at a distance
      of at least 3 nautical miles from the nearest land; or

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a vessel that is in Section I waters
    or Section II waters but not in the inland waters of Canada or a
    designated sewage area, and that is of less than 400 gross tonnage
    and is not certified to carry more than 15 persons,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the sewage is comminuted and disinfected using a
      marine sanitation device and the discharge is made at a distance
      of at least 1 nautical mile from shore,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the discharge is made at a distance of at least
      3 nautical miles from shore while the vessel is en route at the
      fastest feasible speed, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} if it is not feasible to meet the requirements
      of subparagraph (ii) because the vessel is located in waters that
      are less than 6 nautical miles from shore to shore, the discharge
      is made while the vessel is en route at a speed of at least
      4 knots or, if the discharge is not feasible at that speed, the
      discharge is made

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} during an ebb tide, while the vessel is en
        route at the fastest feasible speed and into the deepest waters
        that are located the farthest from shore, or

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} while the vessel is en route at the fastest
        feasible speed and into the deepest and fastest moving waters
        that are located the farthest from shore.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Paragraphs (1)(a) and (b) and
  subparagraphs (1)(c)(ii), (d)(ii) and (e)(i)

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In addition to the circumstances set out in
  paragraphs (1)(a) and (b) and subparagraphs (1)(c)(ii), (d)(ii) and
  (e)(i), the sewage may be discharged only if it does not contain any
  visible solids and the discharge does not cause

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a film or sheen to develop on the water;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a discoloration of the water or its shorelines; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} sewage sludge or an emulsion to be deposited
    beneath the surface of the water or on its shorelines.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Subparagraphs (1)(c)(i), (d)(i) and
  (e)(ii) and (iii)

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In addition to the circumstances set out in
  subparagraphs (1)(c)(i), (d)(i) and (e)(ii) and (iii), the sewage may
  be discharged only if the discharge does not cause visible solids to
  be deposited on the shoreline.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Subparagraph (1)(e)(iii)

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subparagraph (1)(e)(iii) does not apply if a
  reception facility that can receive the sewage in an environmentally
  safe manner is available to receive it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[moderate
  rate]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} In this section, [[moderate
  rate]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a rate that on average over any
  24-hour or shorter period of discharge is not greater than the maximum
  permissible discharge rate calculated in accordance with section 3.1
  of the Annex to the Recommendation on Standards for the Rate of
  Discharge of Untreated Sewage from Ships, IMO Resolution MEPC.157(55),
  and that over any hourly period is not more than 20% greater than that
  rate.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2017-286, s. 33
:::
