#### [SUBDIVISION 6]{.HLabel3}[Operational Testing]{.HTitleText3} {#h-790892 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interpretation

- **[[97]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-97 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  following definitions apply in this section.

  [[biochemical oxygen demand]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[biochemical oxygen demand]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the
      quantity of oxygen determined to be used in the biochemical
      oxidation of organic matter during a five-day period when the
      organic matter is tested in accordance with the method described
      in section 5210 B of the Standard Methods. ([demande biochimique
      en oxygène]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[suspended solids]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[suspended solids]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the total suspended
      solid matter determined to be in or on a liquid when it is tested
      in accordance with the method described in section 2540 D of the
      Standard Methods. ([matières solides en
      suspension]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Testing of effluent

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The authorized representative of a vessel that
  discharges effluent from a marine sanitation device into Section I
  waters must, if the Minister determines that it is necessary to do so
  in order to ascertain whether the effluent meets the specifications on
  the device's certificate of type approval, ensure that samples of the
  effluent are tested in accordance with the Standard Methods to
  determine each of the following that is relevant to those
  specifications:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the fecal coliform count of the samples;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the total suspended solids content of the samples;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the 5-day biochemical oxygen demand of the samples;
    and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in the case of chlorine used as a disinfectant, the
    total residual chlorine content of the samples.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (2) does not apply if the marine
  sanitation device is fitted with instrumentation that meets the
  requirements of subsection (4).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Automatic continuous record

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The instrumentation referred to in subsection (3)
  must indicate the performance of the device by providing an automatic
  continuous record while the device is in operation of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the suspended matter;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the residual disinfectant content, in the case of
    disinfection by chlorine; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the disinfection efficiency, in the case of
    disinfection by any other method.

### [DIVISION 5]{.HLabel2}[Garbage]{.HTitleText2} {#h-790911 .Subheading}

#### [SUBDIVISION 1]{.HLabel3}[General]{.HTitleText3} {#h-790912 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interpretation

**[[98]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-98 .sectionLabel}** The following definitions
apply in this Division.

[[Lake Superior Special Protection Area]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Lake Superior Special Protection Area]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means
    the area enclosed by rhumb lines connecting the following
    coordinates, beginning at the northernmost point and proceeding
    clockwise:

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} 47°30.0′ N, 85°50.0′ W;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} 47°24.2′ N, 85°38.5′ W;

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} 47°04.0′ N, 85°49.0′ W;

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} 47°05.7′ N, 85°59.0′ W;

    - [(e)]{.lawlabel} 47°18.1′ N, 86°05.0′ W. ([zone de protection
      spéciale du lac Supérieur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[Six Fathom Scarp Mid-Lake Special Protection Area]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Six Fathom Scarp Mid-Lake Special Protection
    Area]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the area enclosed by rhumb lines
    connecting the following coordinates, beginning at the northernmost
    point and proceeding clockwise:

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} 44°55′ N, 82°33′ W;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} 44°47′ N, 82°18′ W;

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} 44°39′ N, 82°13′ W;

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} 44°27′ N, 82°13′ W;

    - [(e)]{.lawlabel} 44°27′ N, 82°20′ W;

    - [(f)]{.lawlabel} 44°17′ N, 82°25′ W;

    - [(g)]{.lawlabel} 44°17′ N, 82°30′ W;

    - [(h)]{.lawlabel} 44°28′ N, 82°40′ W;

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} 44°51′ N, 82°44′ W;

    - [(j)]{.lawlabel} 44°53′ N, 82°44′ W;

    - [(k)]{.lawlabel} 44°54′ N, 82°40′ W. ([zone de protection spéciale
      du milieu du lac Six Fathom Scarp]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

**[[99]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-99 .sectionLabel}** This Division does not
apply in respect of vessels in a shipping safety control zone or
Canadian vessels in an area in respect of which subsection 7(3) applies.

#### [SUBDIVISION 2]{.HLabel3}[Discharges of Garbage]{.HTitleText3} {#h-790935 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition

**[[100]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-100 .sectionLabel}** A Canadian vessel in
waters that are not waters under Canadian jurisdiction, and a person on
such a vessel, must not discharge garbage except in accordance with
section 101 or in the circumstances set out in section 5 that apply in
respect of the discharge.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authorized discharge --- garbage

- **[[101]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-101 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purposes of section 187 of the Act and section 100, garbage may be
  discharged from a vessel in Section II waters or a Canadian vessel in
  waters that are not waters under Canadian jurisdiction if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of dunnage, lining material or packing
    material that does not contain plastics and can float, the discharge
    is made as far as feasible from the nearest land and in any case at
    least 25 nautical miles from the nearest land;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} subject to paragraph (c), in the case of garbage
    other than plastics or garbage that is referred to in paragraph (a),
    the discharge is made as far as feasible from the nearest land and
    in any case at least 12 nautical miles from the nearest land;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of garbage that is referred to in
    paragraph (b) and has been passed through a comminuter or grinder
    such that the comminuted or ground garbage can pass through a screen
    with openings not greater than 25 mm, the discharge is made as far
    as feasible from the nearest land and in any case at least
    3 nautical miles from the nearest land; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in the case of cargo residues, the discharge is
    made after all reasonable efforts have been made to empty the cargo
    hold of the cargo residues and to reclaim any cargo residues that
    are on the vessel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply in respect of a vessel
  that is alongside or within 500 m of a fixed or floating platform
  located more than 12 nautical miles from the nearest land and engaged
  in the exploration, exploitation and associated offshore processing of
  seabed mineral resources unless the garbage is food wastes that have
  been passed through a comminuter or grinder such that the comminuted
  or ground food wastes can pass through a screen with openings not
  greater than 25 mm.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authorized discharge --- cargo residues

- **[[102]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-102 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purposes of section 187 of the Act, subject to subsections (2) to
  (4), cargo residues that are garbage may be discharged

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} from a vessel in Lake Ontario, or in Lake Erie east
    of a line that runs due south from Point Pelee, if the discharge is
    made

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} at a distance of more than 12 nautical miles from
      shore, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in the case of iron ore cargo residues, at a
      distance of more than 5.2 nautical miles from shore;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} from a vessel in Lake Erie, in the dredged
    navigation channels running between Toledo Harbor Light and Detroit
    River Light, if the vessel loaded cargo from a Lake Erie port
    immediately after unloading iron ore, coal or salt at that port and
    the cargo residues are residues of the unloaded iron ore, coal or
    salt;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} from a vessel in Lake Huron, other than in the Six
    Fathom Scarp Mid-Lake Special Protection Area, if the discharge is
    made

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} at a distance of more than 12 nautical miles from
      shore, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in the case of iron ore cargo residues, at a
      distance of more than 5.2 nautical miles from shore;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} from a vessel in Lake Huron, other than in the Six
    Fathom Scarp Mid-Lake Special Protection Area, if

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the vessel is upbound along the Thumb of Michigan
      between 5.04 nautical miles northeast of entrance buoys 11 and 12
      and the track line turn abeam of Harbor Beach, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the cargo residues are iron ore, coal or salt
      and the discharge is made at a distance of more than 2.6 nautical
      miles from shore;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} from a vessel in Lake Superior, other than the Lake
    Superior Special Protection Area, if

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the discharge is made from the vessel at a
      distance of more than 12 nautical miles from shore, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in the case of iron ore cargo residues, the
      discharge is made from the vessel at a distance of more than
      5.2 nautical miles from shore;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} from a vessel in Lake Ontario, Lake Erie, Lake
    Huron, other than in the Six Fathom Scarp Mid-Lake Special
    Protection Area, or Lake Superior, other than the Lake Superior
    Special Protection Area, or in any of the connecting and tributary
    waters of those lakes, if the cargo residues are limestone or other
    clean stone;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} from a vessel in the St. Lawrence River west of Les
    Escoumins, if the vessel is en route and the cargo residues are not
    cargo sweepings;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} from a vessel in the inland waters of Canada in the
    St. Lawrence River east of Les Escoumins, if the vessel is en route
    and the discharge is made at a distance of more than 6 nautical
    miles from shore; and

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} from a vessel in the portion of the St. Lawrence
    River and the Gulf of St. Lawrence that is in Section I waters but
    not in the inland waters of Canada, if the vessel is en route and
    the discharge is made at a distance of more than 12 nautical miles
    from shore.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reasonable efforts

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (1), cargo residues
  may be discharged only if all reasonable efforts have been made to
  empty the cargo hold of the cargo residues and to reclaim any cargo
  residues that are on the vessel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Paragraphs (1)(g) to (i)

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of paragraphs (1)(g) to (i), the
  only cargo residues that may be discharged are alumina, bauxite,
  bentonite, cement, chrome ore, clay, dolomite, ferromanganese, grain,
  gypsum, ilmenite, iron ore, iron ore concentrate, lead ore
  concentrate, limestone, manganese concentrate, manganese ore,
  nepheline syenite, perlite, quartz, salt, sand, stone, sugar, talc,
  urea, vermiculite and zinc ore concentrate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Nearby marine mammals

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply in respect of a vessel
  when its master or a crew member ascertains by visual observation that
  a marine mammal is within 0.5 nautical miles of the vessel.

- Definition of [[grain]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} In subsection (3), [[grain]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}
  means wheat, corn, oats, rye, barley, flax, soybeans, safflower,
  canola, rice, pulses and other seeds, and the processed form of seeds,
  including seedcake and cereal meals.

#### [SUBDIVISION 3]{.HLabel3}[Placards and Garbage Management Plans]{.HTitleText3} {#h-790975 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Display of placards

- **[[103]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-103
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every vessel of 12 m or more in
  length overall must display placards that notify the crew and
  passengers of the garbage discharge requirements of section 187 of the
  Act and sections 7 and 100 to 102, as applicable.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Language

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The placards must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a Canadian vessel or a Canadian
    pleasure craft, be written in English or French or in both,
    according to the needs of the crew and the passengers; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a foreign vessel or a foreign
    pleasure craft, be written in the working language of the crew and
    in English, French or Spanish.

- Definition of [[length overall]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In this section, [[length
  overall]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}, in respect of a vessel, means the
  distance measured from the forward end of the foremost outside surface
  of the hull shell to the aft end of the aftermost outside surface of
  the hull shell.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Keep on board garbage management plans

- **[[104]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-104
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every vessel of 400 gross tonnage or
  more or that is certified to carry 15 persons or more must keep on
  board a garbage management plan that meets the requirements of
  regulation 9(2) of Annex V to MARPOL.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Crew members' obligation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every crew member must meet the applicable
  requirements of the plan.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Language

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), in the case of a Canadian
  vessel or a Canadian pleasure craft, the garbage management plan must
  be written in English or French or in both, according to the needs of
  the crew.

#### [SUBDIVISION 4]{.HLabel3}[Record-keeping]{.HTitleText3} {#h-790992 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Garbage Record Books

- **[[105]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-105
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every vessel of 400 gross tonnage or
  more or that is certified to carry 15 persons or more must keep on
  board a Garbage Record Book in the form set out in the appendix to
  Annex V to MARPOL.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply in respect of a vessel
  that is certified to carry 15 persons or more and engages only on
  voyages of one hour or less.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Entries --- officer in charge

- **[[106]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-106 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  officer in charge of an operation referred to in regulation 9(3) of
  Annex V to MARPOL that takes place on a vessel in respect of which
  section 105 applies must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} ensure that the operation is recorded without delay
    in the Garbage Record Book; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} sign the recorded entry.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Entries --- master

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The master of a vessel in respect of which
  section 105 applies must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} ensure that the circumstances of and reasons for
    any discharge referred to in paragraph 5(a), (d) or (e), or any
    other accidental or exceptional discharge, is recorded without delay
    in the Garbage Record Book;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} ensure that each entry recorded in the Garbage
    Record Book is signed by the officer in charge of the operation; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} sign each page of the Garbage Record Book after the
    page is completed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Two years

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The vessel must keep the Garbage Record Book on board
  for two years after the day on which the last entry was made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Language

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An entry in the Garbage Record Book must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a Canadian vessel or a Canadian
    pleasure craft, be written in English or French; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a foreign vessel or a foreign
    pleasure craft, be written in English, French or Spanish.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Categories of garbage

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (6), garbage must be
  grouped into the following categories:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} plastics (Category 1);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} dunnage, lining material or packing material
    referred to in paragraph 101(1)(a) (Category 2);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} garbage referred to in paragraph 101(1)(c), other
    than food wastes and incinerator ash (Category 3);

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} garbage referred to in paragraph 101(1)(b), other
    than food wastes and incinerator ash (Category 4);

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} food wastes (Category 5); and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} incinerator ash, except incinerator ash from
    plastics that may contain toxic or heavy-metal residues
    (Category 6).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Categories

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The master of a vessel shall ensure that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} garbage that is discharged in accordance with
    section 101 or 102 is listed in the Garbage Record Book under the
    heading for Category 2, 3, 4, 5 or 6, as the case may be; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} garbage that is transferred to a reception facility
    is

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in the case of plastic, is listed in the Garbage
      Record Book under the heading for Category 1, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, is listed in the Garbage
      Record Book under the heading for "other".

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Official log book

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The Garbage Record Book may be part of the vessel's
  official log book.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reception facility receipts

- **[[107]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-107 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  master of a vessel must obtain, from the owner or operator of a
  reception facility that receives garbage from the vessel, a receipt or
  certificate that sets out the type and amount of garbage received and
  the date and time that it was received.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}One year

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The master must keep the receipt or certificate on
  board for one year after the day on which it was issued.

### [DIVISION 6]{.HLabel2}[Air]{.HTitleText2} {#h-791035 .Subheading}

#### [SUBDIVISION 1]{.HLabel3}[Requirements for Control of Emissions from Vessels]{.HTitleText3} {#h-791036 .Subheading}

##### [Plans and Specifications]{.HTitleText4} {#h-791037 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Approval

**[[108]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-108 .sectionLabel}** On application, the
Minister must approve plans and specifications with respect to a
Canadian vessel or a vessel that is recorded under the Act if the
matters described in the plans and specifications meet the applicable
requirements of this Subdivision.

##### [Ozone-depleting Substances]{.HTitleText4} {#h-791040 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Emission prohibited

- **[[109]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-109 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  vessel must not emit and a person must not permit the emission of an
  ozone-depleting substance from an installation on a vessel except in
  the circumstances set out in section 5 that apply in respect of the
  emission.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply in respect of a minimal
  emission of an ozone-depleting substance if the emission is associated
  with the recapture or recycling of an ozone-depleting substance.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Systems, etc.

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The authorized representative of a vessel must ensure
  that it is not fitted with any system, equipment --- including
  portable fire-extinguishing units --- insulation or other material
  that contains an ozone-depleting substance.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application --- vessels constructed
  before May 19, 2005

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} In the case of a vessel that is constructed before
  May 19, 2005, subsection (3) does not apply in respect of any system,
  equipment, insulation or other material that contains an
  ozone-depleting substance and that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is fitted before that date; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the contractual delivery date of the system,
    equipment, insulation or other material to the vessel is before that
    date, is fitted at any time.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application --- vessels constructed
  before January 1, 2020

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} In the case of a vessel that is constructed before
  January 1, 2020, subsection (3) does not apply in respect of any
  system, equipment, insulation or other material that contains a
  hydrochlorofluorocarbon but no other ozone-depleting substance and
  that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is fitted before that date; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the contractual delivery date of the system,
    equipment, insulation or other material to the vessel is before that
    date, is fitted at any time.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application --- repair or recharge

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (3) does not apply in respect of the
  repair or recharge of any system, equipment, insulation or other
  material to which subsection (4) or (5) applies.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application --- permanently sealed
  equipment

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} This section does not apply in respect of permanently
  sealed equipment that has no refrigerant-charging connections or
  potentially removable components containing ozone depleting
  substances.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2013-68, s. 14
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-109-20120330.html "Link to previous version of section 109"){.PITLink}
:::

##### [Nitrogen Oxides (NO~x~) --- Marine Diesel Engines]{.HTitleText4} {#h-791062 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

**[[110]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-110 .sectionLabel}** Sections 110.1 to 110.3
do not apply in respect of a marine diesel engine that is

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} intended to be used solely for emergencies;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} intended to be used solely to power any device or
  equipment that is intended to be used solely for emergencies on the
  vessel on which the device or equipment is installed; or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} installed in a lifeboat that is intended to be used
  solely for emergencies.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2013-68, s. 15
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-110-20120330.html "Link to previous version of section 110"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Tier I --- power output of more than 130
kW

- **[[110.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-110.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} This section applies in respect of a
  marine diesel engine that has a power output of more than 130 kW and
  that is installed on

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a Canadian vessel or a Canadian pleasure craft that
    was constructed after December 31, 1999 but before January 1, 2011
    and that does not engage only on voyages in waters under Canadian
    jurisdiction;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a foreign vessel or a foreign pleasure craft that
    was constructed after December 31, 1999 but before January 1, 2011;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a Canadian vessel or a Canadian pleasure craft that
    was constructed after May 2, 2007 but before the day on which this
    section comes into force and that engages only on voyages in waters
    under Canadian jurisdiction;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a Canadian vessel or a Canadian pleasure craft that
    was constructed before January 1, 2000 and that does not engage only
    on voyages in waters under Canadian jurisdiction, or a foreign
    vessel or a foreign pleasure craft that was constructed before
    January 1, 2000, if

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} after December 31, 1999 but before January 1,
      2011,

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} the engine replaced a marine diesel engine that
        is not identical to the engine, or

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} the engine was installed as an additional
        engine,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} after December 31, 1999, a substantial
      modification, as defined in section 1.3.2 of the NO~x~ Technical
      Code, is made to the engine, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} after December 31, 1999, the maximum continuous
      rating of the engine is increased by more than 10%; or

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a Canadian vessel or a Canadian pleasure craft that
    was constructed before May 3, 2007 and that engages only on voyages
    in waters under Canadian jurisdiction, if

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} after May 2, 2007 but before the day on which
      this section comes into force,

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} the engine replaced a marine diesel engine that
        is not identical to the engine and that was installed on the
        vessel before May 3, 2007, or

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} the engine was installed as an additional
        engine, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} after May 2, 2007,

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} a substantial modification, as defined in
        section 1.3.2 of the NO~x~ Technical Code, is made to the
        engine, or

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} the maximum continuous rating of the engine is
        increased by more than 10%.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Tier I --- power output of more than 5
  000 kW

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} This section applies in respect of a marine diesel
  engine that has a power output of more than 5 000 kW and a
  displacement of 90 L or more per cylinder, and that is installed on

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a Canadian vessel or a Canadian pleasure craft that
    was constructed after December 31, 1989 but before May 3, 2007 and
    that engages only on voyages in waters under Canadian jurisdiction;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a Canadian vessel or a Canadian pleasure craft that
    was constructed after December 31, 1989 but before January 1, 2000
    and that does not engage only on voyages in waters under Canadian
    jurisdiction; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a foreign vessel or a foreign pleasure craft that
    was constructed after December 31, 1989 but before January 1, 2000.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Emission limits

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to sections 110.5 and 110.6 and, in the case
  of a marine diesel engine to which subsection (2) applies, regulations
  13.7.1 to 13.7.3 of Annex VI to MARPOL, the authorized representative
  of a vessel must ensure that a marine diesel engine is not operated on
  the vessel if the quantity of nitrogen oxides emitted from the engine,
  calculated as the total weighted emission of NO~2~, exceeds the
  following limits, where n represents the rated engine speed
  (crankshaft revolutions per minute) of the engine:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} 17.0 g/kWh, where n is less than 130 revolutions
    per minute;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} 45.0 × n^-0.2^ g/kWh, where n is 130 revolutions
    per minute or more but less than 2,000 revolutions per minute; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} 9.8 g/kWh, where n is 2,000 revolutions per minute
    or more.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificates

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} [In the case of a Canadian vessel that engages only
  on voyages in waters under Canadian jurisdiction, a reference in
  regulation 13.7.1 of Annex VI to MARPOL to the vessel's International
  Air Pollution Prevention Certificate is to be read as a reference to
  the vessel's Canadian Air Pollution Prevention
  Certificate.]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2013-68, s. 15
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Tier II

- **[[110.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-110.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} This section applies in respect of a
  marine diesel engine that has a power output of more than 130 kW and
  that is installed on

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a vessel that is constructed after December 31,
    2010, other than a Canadian vessel or a Canadian pleasure craft that
    was constructed before the day on which this section comes into
    force and that engages only on voyages in waters under Canadian
    jurisdiction;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a vessel, other than a Canadian vessel or a
    Canadian pleasure craft that was constructed before the day on which
    this section comes into force and that engages only on voyages in
    waters under Canadian jurisdiction, if

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the vessel was constructed before January 1,
      2011, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} after December 31, 2010,

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} the engine replaces a marine diesel engine that
        is not identical to the engine and that was installed on the
        vessel before January 1, 2011, or

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} the engine is installed as an additional
        engine; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a Canadian vessel or a Canadian pleasure craft that
    engages only on voyages in waters under Canadian jurisdiction, if

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the vessel was constructed before the day on
      which this section comes into force, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} on or after the day on which this section comes
      into force,

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} the engine replaces a marine diesel engine that
        is not identical to the engine and that was installed on the
        vessel before the day on which this section comes into force, or

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} the engine is installed as an additional
        engine.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} This section does not apply in respect of a marine
  diesel engine to which section 110.3 applies.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Emission limits

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to sections 110.5 and 110.6, the authorized
  representative of a vessel must ensure that a marine diesel engine is
  not operated on the vessel if the quantity of nitrogen oxides emitted
  from the engine, calculated as the total weighted emission of NO~2~,
  exceeds the following limits, where n represents the rated engine
  speed (crankshaft revolutions per minute) of the engine:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} 14.4 g/kWh, where n is less than 130 revolutions
    per minute;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} 44.0 × n^-0.23^ g/kWh, where n is 130 revolutions
    per minute or more but less than 2,000 revolutions per minute; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} 7.7 g/kWh, where n is 2,000 revolutions per minute
    or more.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2013-68, s. 15
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Tier III

- **[[110.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-110.3
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} This section applies in respect of a
  marine diesel engine that has a power output of more than 130 kW and
  that is installed on

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a vessel that is constructed on or after January 1,
    2016; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a vessel that is constructed before January 1, 2016
    if, on or after January 1, 2016,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the engine replaces a marine diesel engine that
      is not identical to the engine and that was installed on the
      vessel before January 1, 2016, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the engine is installed as an additional engine.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- marine diesel engines
  installed on certain vessels

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} This section does not apply in respect of a marine
  diesel engine that is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} installed on a vessel with a length less than 24 m
    that has been specifically designed for, and is used solely for,
    recreational purposes;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} installed on a vessel with a combined nameplate
    diesel engine propulsion power of less than 750 kW, if it is not
    possible for the engine to meet the requirements of subsection (4)
    because of design or construction limitations of the vessel;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} installed on a vessel after December 31, 2015 as a
    replacement for a marine diesel engine that is not identical to the
    engine, if it is not possible for the engine to meet the
    requirements of subsection (4); or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} installed on a vessel that is entitled to fly the
    flag of the United States.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- vessels operating in
  certain waters

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} This section does not apply in respect of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a Canadian vessel or a Canadian pleasure craft that
    is operating

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in arctic waters, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in waters that are not waters under Canadian
      jurisdiction and are not within an emission control area; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a foreign vessel or a foreign pleasure craft that
    is operating in arctic waters or in Hudson Bay, James Bay or Ungava
    Bay.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Emission limits

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subject to sections 110.5 and 110.6, the authorized
  representative of a vessel must ensure that a marine diesel engine is
  not operated on the vessel if the quantity of nitrogen oxides emitted
  from the engine, calculated as the total weighted emission of NO~2~,
  exceeds the following limits, where n represents the rated engine
  speed (crankshaft revolutions per minute) of the engine:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} 3.4 g/kWh, where n is less than 130 revolutions per
    minute;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} 9.0 × n^-0.2^ g/kWh, where n is 130 revolutions per
    minute or more but less than 2,000 revolutions per minute; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} 2.0 g/kWh, where n is 2,000 revolutions per minute
    or more.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Change of date

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If the IMO, in accordance with regulation 13.10 of
  Annex VI to MARPOL, sets a later date for the purposes of regulation
  5.1.1 of that Annex, the references in subsection (1) to January 1,
  2016 are to be read as references to that later date.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2013-68, s. 15
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determining quantity of nitrogen oxides

**[[110.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-110.4 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
subsections 110.1(3), 110.2(3) and 110.3(4), the quantity of nitrogen
oxides emitted must be determined in accordance with the NO~x~ Technical
Code.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2013-68, s. 15
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exhaust gas cleaning systems

**[[110.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-110.5 .sectionLabel}** A marine diesel
engine may be operated if an exhaust gas cleaning system or any other
equivalent method is used to reduce the quantity of nitrogen oxides
emissions to no more than the limits specified in subsection 110.1(3),
110.2(3) or 110.3(4), as the case may be.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2013-68, s. 15
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exceptions to prohibited emissions

**[[110.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-110.6 .sectionLabel}** Nitrogen oxides may
be emitted in the circumstances set out in section 5 that apply in
respect of the emission.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2013-68, s. 15
:::

##### [Sulphur Oxides (SO~x~)]{.HTitleText4} {#h-791166 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Maximum sulphur content of fuel oil

- **[[111]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-111
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (2) to (5)
  and section 111.1, the authorized representative of a vessel must
  ensure that the sulphur content of the fuel oil used on board the
  vessel does not exceed

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} 3.50% by mass before January 1, 2020, in the case
    of a foreign vessel or a foreign pleasure craft that is operating in
    arctic waters or in Hudson Bay, James Bay or Ungava Bay;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} 3.50% by mass before January 1, 2020, in the case
    of a Canadian vessel or a Canadian pleasure craft that is operating
    in arctic waters or in Hudson Bay, James Bay or Ungava Bay;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} 3.50% by mass before January 1, 2020, in the case
    of a Canadian vessel that is operating in waters that are not waters
    under Canadian jurisdiction and that are not in an emission control
    area;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} 0.50% by mass after December 31, 2019, in the case
    of a foreign vessel or a foreign pleasure craft that is operating in
    arctic waters or in Hudson Bay, James Bay or Ungava Bay;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} 0.50% by mass after December 31, 2019, in the case
    of a Canadian vessel or a Canadian pleasure craft that is operating
    in arctic waters or in Hudson Bay, James Bay or Ungava Bay;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} 0.50% by mass after December 31, 2019, in the case
    of a Canadian vessel that is operating in waters that are not waters
    under Canadian jurisdiction and that are not in an emission control
    area;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} 1.00% by mass before January 1, 2015, in the case
    of a Canadian vessel or a Canadian pleasure craft that is operating
    in waters under Canadian jurisdiction other than arctic waters;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} 1.00% by mass before January 1, 2015, in the case
    of a Canadian vessel that is operating in waters that are not waters
    under Canadian jurisdiction and that are in an emission control
    area;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} 1.00% by mass before January 1, 2015, in the case
    of a foreign vessel or a foreign pleasure craft that is operating in
    waters under Canadian jurisdiction other than arctic waters or in
    Hudson Bay, James Bay or Ungava Bay;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} 0.10% by mass after December 31, 2014, in the case
    of a Canadian vessel or a Canadian pleasure craft that is operating
    in waters under Canadian jurisdiction other than arctic waters;

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} 0.10% by mass after December 31, 2014, in the case
    of a Canadian vessel that is operating in waters that are not waters
    under Canadian jurisdiction and that are in an emission control
    area; and

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} 0.10% by mass after December 31, 2014, in the case
    of a foreign vessel or a foreign pleasure craft that is operating in
    waters under Canadian jurisdiction other than arctic waters or in
    Hudson Bay, James Bay or Ungava Bay.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Steam-powered foreign vessels and
  foreign pleasure craft

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (3) and (4), in the case of a
  foreign vessel or a foreign pleasure craft that is powered by a
  propulsion boiler that was not originally designed for continued
  operation on marine distillate fuel or natural gas, the vessel's
  authorized representative must ensure that, when the vessel is
  operating in the North American Emission Control Area or in the Great
  Lakes and St. Lawrence waters, the sulphur content of the fuel oil
  used on board the vessel does not exceed

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} 3.50% by mass before January 1, 2020; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} 0.50% by mass after December 31, 2019.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsections (1) and (2) do not apply in respect of a
  foreign vessel or a foreign pleasure craft that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is powered by a propulsion boiler that was not
    originally designed for continued operation on marine distillate
    fuel or natural gas; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} operates solely on the Great Lakes and their
    connecting waters.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Alternative measure

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Instead of meeting the requirements of subsection (1)
  or (2), the authorized representative of a vessel may ensure that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the vessel operates an exhaust gas cleaning system
    that meets the requirements of Resolution MEPC.184(59); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the emissions of sulphur oxides produced by the
    operation of the system do not exceed the emissions that would be
    produced were fuel oil with the sulphur content by mass required by
    that subsection used on board the vessel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When different fuel is ussed

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The master of a vessel referred to in subparagraph
  122(1)(a)(ii) or (iii) must ensure that the requirements of regulation
  14.6 of Annex VI to MARPOL are met if the vessel is entering or
  leaving an emission control area and the fuel oil used on board within
  the area is different from the fuel oil used on board outside the
  area.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Residues from exhaust gas cleaning
  systems

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} If a vessel operates an exhaust gas cleaning system
  that has been certified in accordance with Resolution MEPC.184(59),
  the vessel's authorized representative must ensure that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any exhaust gas cleaning system residues are
    delivered to an onshore reception facility; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the washwater from the operation of the system, as
    well as the monitoring and recording of the washwater, meets the
    requirements of section 10 of the Resolution.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2013-68, s. 15
- SOR/2013-235, s. 37(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-111-20130418.html "Link to previous version of section 111"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

- **[[111.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-111.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} This section, instead of section
  111, applies in respect of an authorized representative's Canadian
  vessels when they are operating in the Great Lakes and St. Lawrence
  waters during the period referred to in paragraph (4)(a) or during a
  year referred to in subsection (4) if, before the period or year
  begins, the authorized representative

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} notifies the Minister that the authorized
    representative elects to have this section apply in respect of that
    period or year; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} provides the Minister with a report that specifies
    the manner in which each of the vessels will be managed for the
    purposes of meeting the requirements of subsection (4) or (6) for
    that period or year.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application --- alternative

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} This section, instead of section 111, applies in
  respect of an authorized representative's Canadian vessels when they
  are operating in the Great Lakes and St. Lawrence waters during the
  period beginning on the day on which this section comes into force and
  ending on December 31, 2020 if the authorized representative

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} before the period begins, notifies the Minister
    that the authorized representative elects to have this section apply
    in respect of that period; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} before the period referred to in paragraph (5)(a)
    and before each year referred to in column 3 of the table to
    subsection (5), provides the Minister with a report that specifies
    the manner in which each of the vessels will be managed for the
    purposes of meeting the requirements of subsection (5) or (6) for
    that period or year.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fuel oil used in other waters under
  Canadian jurisdiction

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In the notification, the vessels' authorized
  representative may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for the purposes of calculating the total amount of
    fuel oil used on board the vessels, elect to include the fuel oil
    used on board any of the vessels when they are operating in waters
    under Canadian jurisdiction that are not within the Great Lakes and
    St. Lawrence waters; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} for the purposes of calculating the average sulphur
    content by mass of the total amount of fuel oil used on board the
    vessels, elect not to include

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} 10% of the sulphur content by mass of the fuel
      oil used on board any of the vessels that were first delivered
      after December 31, 2008 but before August 1, 2012, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} 20% of the sulphur content by mass of the fuel
      oil used on board any of the vessels that were first delivered
      after July 31, 2012 or on which a marine diesel engine that has a
      power output of more than 5 000 kW was installed after July 31,
      2012.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Average sulphur content

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If an election is made under subsection (1), the
  vessels' authorized representative must ensure that the average
  sulphur content by mass of the total amount of fuel oil used on board
  the vessels does not exceed

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} 1.30% in the period beginning on the day on which
    this section comes into force and ending on December 31, 2013;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} 1.20% in 2014;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} 1.10% in 2015;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} 1.00% in 2016;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} 0.80% in 2017;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} 0.60% in 2018;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} 0.40% in 2019; and

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} 0.10% in 2020.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Average sulphur content and cumulative
  average sulphur content

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If an election is made under subsection (2), the
  vessels' authorized representative must ensure that the average
  sulphur content by mass of the total amount of fuel oil used on board
  the vessels does not exceed

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} 1.70% during the period that begins on the day on
    which this section comes into force and ends on December 31, 2013;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the amount set out in column 1 of the table to this
    subsection during the year set out in column 3; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the amount set out in column 2 of the table to this
    subsection during the period that begins on the day on which this
    section comes into force and ends on December 31 of the year set out
    in column 3.
