- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Alternative measures

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Instead of meeting the requirements of subsection (4)
  or (5), the vessels' authorized representative may ensure that any
  combination of the following on one or more of the vessels results in
  total emissions of sulphur oxides that do not exceed the total
  emissions of sulphur oxides that would be produced were fuel oil with
  the sulphur content by mass required by that subsection used on board
  the vessels:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the operation of an exhaust gas cleaning system
    that meets the requirements of Resolution MEPC.184(59);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the use of equipment or materials or the carrying
    out of procedures; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the use of fuel oil with a reduced sulphur content.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Washwater from exhaust gas cleaning
  systems

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The vessels' authorized representative must ensure
  that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any exhaust gas cleaning system residues are
    delivered to an onshore reception facility that is licensed by the
    jurisdiction where the facility is located; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if one or more of the vessels operate an exhaust
    gas cleaning system that has been certified in accordance with
    Resolution MEPC.184(59), the washwater from the operation of the
    system, as well as the monitoring and recording of the washwater,
    meets the requirement of section 10 of the Resolution.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report --- the manner in which vessels
  will be managed

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} The vessels' authorized representative must provide
  the Minister with a revised report as soon as feasible if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} after a report is provided under paragraph (1)(b),
    the manner in which any of the vessels are managed in order to meet
    the requirements of subsection (4) or (6) changes; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} after a report is provided under paragraph (2)(b),
    the manner in which any of the vessels are managed in order to meet
    the requirements of subsection (5) or (6) changes.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interim report --- the manner in which
  vessels are being managed

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} The vessels' authorized representative must, during
  the period beginning on June 1 and ending on September 30 of any year
  in respect of which an election is made under subsection (1) or (2),
  provide the Minister with an interim report that describes how each of
  the vessels is being managed in order to meet the requirements of
  subsection (4), (5) or (6) for that year.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report --- how vessels were managed

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} The vessels' authorized representative must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if an election is made under subsection (1) in
    respect of a period or year, provide the Minister, on or before
    March 1 of the year following the period or year, with a report that
    describes the manner in which each of the vessels was managed in
    order to meet the requirements of subsection (4) or (6) for that
    period or year; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if an election is made under subsection (2),
    provide the Minister, on or before March 1 of each year starting in
    2014 and ending in 2021, with a report that describes the manner in
    which each of the vessels was managed in order to meet the
    requirements of subsection (5) or (6) for

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the period that begins on the day on which this
      section comes into force and ends on December 31, 2013, in the
      case of a report made in 2014, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the year before the report is made, in any other
      case.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Auditing

  [(11)]{.lawlabel} The reports referred to in subsection (10) must be
  audited for accuracy by a person who has knowledge of the methods of
  conducting audits and is independent of the authorized representative.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Canadian Air Pollution Prevention
  Certificates

  [(12)]{.lawlabel} Despite paragraph 122(1)(a), if an election is made
  under paragraph (1)(a) or (2)(a) in respect of a vessel, the vessel

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} must hold and keep on board a Canadian Air
    Pollution Prevention Certificate; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is not required to hold and keep on board an
    International Air Pollution Prevention Certificate, unless the
    vessel operates in waters that are not waters under Canadian
    jurisdiction and are not within the Great Lakes and St. Lawrence
    waters.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2013-68, s. 15
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Documentation if exhaust gas cleaning
system is operated

**[[111.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-111.2 .sectionLabel}** If a vessel
operates an exhaust gas cleaning system referred to in paragraph
111(4)(a) or 111.1(6)(a) or (c),

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the vessel must hold and keep on board a certificate
  of type approval certifying that the system meets the applicable
  requirements referred to in Resolution MEPC.184(59);

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the vessel must keep on board an EGC System Technical
  Manual "Scheme A" that meets the requirements of section 4.2.2 of
  Resolution MEPC.184(59) or an EGC System Technical Manual "Scheme B"
  that meets the requirements of section 5.6 of Resolution MEPC.184(59);

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the vessel must keep on board a SO~x~ Emissions
  Compliance Plan that meets the requirements of section 9.1.1 of
  Resolution MEPC.184(59);

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the authorized representative must ensure that the
  information required by Resolution MEPC.184(59) respecting the
  operation, maintenance, servicing, adjustments and monitoring of the
  system is recorded as required by the Resolution; and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the vessel must keep on board the information
  referred to in paragraph (d) in the form and manner required by
  Resolution MEPC.184(59).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2013-68, s. 15
:::

##### [Diesel Engines with a Displacement of Less than 30 L Per Cylinder]{.HTitleText4} {#h-791276 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}New diesel engines

- **[[111.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-111.3
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The authorized representative of a
  Canadian vessel or a Canadian pleasure craft must ensure that any new
  diesel engine that has a displacement of 7 L or more per cylinder but
  less than 30 L per cylinder, and that is installed on the vessel for
  its propulsion, has been certified

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} by the United States Environmental Protection
    Agency as meeting the requirements of Title 40, section 1042.101, of
    the Code of Federal Regulations of the United States for Category 2
    engines; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} by the government of another state as meeting
    requirements for emissions of particulate matter, nitrogen oxides
    and hydrocarbons that are equivalent to the requirements referred to
    in paragraph (a).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deferred application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply before January 1, 2016.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2013-68, s. 15
:::

##### [Volatile Organic Compounds]{.HTitleText4} {#h-791286 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Vapour collection systems

- **[[112]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-112 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  authorized representative of an oil tanker, an NLS tanker or a gas
  carrier that uses a vapour collection system for volatile organic
  compounds must ensure that the vessel is fitted with a vapour
  collection system that meets the requirements of regulation 15.5 of
  Annex VI to MARPOL.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application to gas carriers

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) applies in respect of a gas carrier
  only if the type of loading and containment systems used by the
  carrier allow safe retention of non-methane volatile organic compounds
  on board or their safe return ashore.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}VOC management plan

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The authorized representative of a crude oil tanker
  must ensure that a volatile organic compounds management plan that
  meets the requirements of regulation 15.6 of Annex VI to MARPOL is
  implemented.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2013-68, s. 16
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-112-20120330.html "Link to previous version of section 112"){.PITLink}
:::

##### [Shipboard Incineration]{.HTitleText4} {#h-791296 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition

**[[113]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-113 .sectionLabel}** A person must not
incinerate any of the following substances on a vessel:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} oil cargo residues, noxious liquid substance cargo
  residues and marine pollutants;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} polychlorinated biphenyls;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} garbage containing more than traces of heavy metals;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} refined petroleum products containing halogen
  compounds;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} sewage sludge and sludge oil that are not generated
  on board the vessel; and

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} exhaust gas cleaning system residues.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2013-68, s. 17
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-113-20120330.html "Link to previous version of section 113"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition unless in a shipboard
incinerator

- **[[114]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-114
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), a person
  must not incinerate a substance on a vessel unless the incineration is
  in a shipboard incinerator.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sewage sludge or sludge oil

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Sewage sludge or sludge oil generated during the
  normal operation of a vessel may be incinerated in the main or
  auxiliary power plant or the boilers on the vessel if the incineration
  does not take place inside ports, harbours or estuaries.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Polyvinyl chlorides

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A person must not incinerate polyvinyl chlorides on a
  vessel unless the incineration is in a shipboard incinerator that
  meets the requirements of regulation 16.6.1 of Annex VI to MARPOL.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Shipboard incinerators

- **[[115]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-115 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} This
  section applies in respect of a shipboard incinerator that is
  installed

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} after December 31, 1999

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} on a Canadian vessel that does not engage only on
      voyages in waters under Canadian jurisdiction, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} on a foreign vessel; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} after May 2, 2007 on a Canadian vessel that engages
    only on voyages in waters under Canadian jurisdiction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulation 16.6.1 of Annex VI to MARPOL

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The authorized representative of a vessel must ensure
  that every shipboard incinerator installed on the vessel meets the
  requirements of regulation 16.6.1 of Annex VI to MARPOL.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Operating personnel

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The authorized representative of a vessel must ensure
  that the personnel responsible for the operation of a shipboard
  incinerator are trained and capable of implementing the guidance
  provided in the manufacturer's operating manual.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Monitoring

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The master of a vessel must ensure that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the combustion flue gas outlet temperature of a
    shipboard incinerator is monitored at all times; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} waste or other matter is not fed into a
    continuous-feed shipboard incinerator when the temperature is below
    850°C.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Batch-loaded shipboard incinerators

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The authorized representative of a vessel on which a
  batch-loaded shipboard incinerator is installed must ensure that it is
  designed so that the temperature in the combustion chamber reaches
  600°C within five minutes after start-up and stabilizes at not less
  than 850°C.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2013-68, s. 18
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-115-20120330.html "Link to previous version of section 115"){.PITLink}
:::

##### [Fuel Oil Quality]{.HTitleText4} {#h-791333 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirements

- **[[116]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-116 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  authorized representative of a vessel must ensure that fuel oil used
  on board the vessel for combustion purposes does not contain inorganic
  acid and meets the following requirements:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of fuel oil derived from petroleum
    refining, the fuel oil must be a blend of hydrocarbons, with or
    without the incorporation of small amounts of additives that are
    intended to improve performance, and must not contain any added
    substance or chemical waste that

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} jeopardizes the vessel's safety,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} jeopardizes the safety or health of the vessel's
      personnel,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} adversely affects the performance of the
      vessel's machinery, or

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} contributes overall to additional air pollution;
      or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of fuel oil derived by methods other
    than petroleum refining, the fuel oil must not

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} jeopardize the vessel's safety,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} jeopardize the safety or health of the vessel's
      personnel,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} adversely affect the performance of the
      vessel's machinery, or

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} contribute overall to additional air pollution.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limited application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subparagraphs (1)(a)(ii) and (iii) and (b)(ii)
  and (iii) apply in respect of foreign vessels, and pleasure craft that
  are not Canadian vessels, only when they are in Canadian waters.

##### [Unavailability of Compliant Fuel Oil]{.HTitleText4} {#h-791349 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Canadian vessels and Canadian pleasure
craft

- **[[116.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-116.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a Canadian vessel or a Canadian
  pleasure craft cannot, while voyaging in accordance with its voyage
  plan, obtain fuel oil that meets the requirements of this Division,
  its master must notify the Minister and, if its port of destination is
  not in Canada, the competent authority of that port.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Foreign vessels and foreign pleasure
  craft

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a foreign vessel or a foreign pleasure craft whose
  port of destination is in Canada cannot, while voyaging in accordance
  with its voyage plan, obtain fuel oil that meets the requirements of
  this Division, its master must notify the Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of notification

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The notification must include

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the vessel's name and, if applicable, the vessel's
    IMO ship identification number;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the vessel's port of origin and port of
    destination;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} details of the attempts that were made to obtain
    fuel oil that meets the requirements of this Division, including the
    names and addresses of the fuel oil suppliers contacted, and the
    dates on which contact was made;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the sulphur content of the fuel oil that was
    obtained; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the measures that will be taken to obtain, as soon
    as feasible, fuel oil that meets the requirements of this Division.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2013-68, s. 19
:::

##### [Energy Efficiency]{.HTitleText4} {#h-791364 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interpretation

- **[[116.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-116.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The following definitions apply in
  this section.

  [[bulk carrier]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[bulk carrier]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a vessel that is
      intended primarily to carry dry cargo in bulk, but does not
      include combination carriers. ([vraquier]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[combination carrier]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[combination carrier]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a vessel
      designed to carry liquid or dry cargo in bulk. ([transporteur
      mixte]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[container vessel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[container vessel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a vessel designed
      exclusively for the carriage of
      containers. ([porte-conteneurs]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[existing vessel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[existing vessel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a vessel that is not
      a new vessel. ([bâtiment existant]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[gas carrier]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[gas carrier]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a cargo vessel
      constructed or adapted, and used, for the carriage in bulk of any
      liquefied gas. ([transporteur de gaz]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[general cargo vessel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[general cargo vessel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a vessel with a
      multi-deck or single deck hull designed primarily for the carriage
      of general cargo, but does not include livestock carriers, barge
      carriers, heavy load carriers, yacht carriers or nuclear fuel
      carriers. ([bâtiment pour marchandises diverses]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[new vessel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[new vessel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a vessel

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for which the building contract is placed after
        June 30, 2013;

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that is constructed after June 30, 2013, in the
        absence of a building contract; or

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} that is delivered 30 months or more after June
        30, 2015. ([bâtiment neuf]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[refrigerated cargo carrier]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[refrigerated cargo carrier]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a vessel
      designed exclusively for the carriage of refrigerated cargoes in
      holds. ([transporteur de cargaisons réfrigérées]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[ro-ro cargo vessel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[ro-ro cargo vessel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a vessel designed
      for the carriage of cargo transportation units. ([bâtiment de
      charge roulier]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[ro-ro cargo vessel (vehicle carrier)]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[ro-ro cargo vessel (vehicle carrier)]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means
      a multi-deck ro-ro cargo vessel designed for the carriage of empty
      cars and trucks. ([bâtiment de charge roulier (transporteur de
      véhicules)]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[ro-ro passenger vessel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[ro-ro passenger vessel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a passenger
      vessel with ro-ro cargo spaces. ([bâtiment roulier à
      passagers]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[tanker]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[tanker]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a chemical tanker, NLS tanker
      or oil tanker. ([bâtiment-citerne]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsections (3) and (4) do not apply in respect of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a Canadian vessel that engages only on voyages in
    waters under Canadian jurisdiction or in the Great Lakes and St.
    Lawrence waters; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a vessel that has a diesel-electric propulsion,
    turbine propulsion or hybrid propulsion system.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Attained Energy Efficiency Design Index

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In the case of a vessel of 400 gross tonnage or more
  that is a bulk carrier, combination carrier, container vessel, gas
  carrier, general cargo vessel, passenger vessel, refrigerated cargo
  carrier, ro-ro cargo vessel, ro-ro cargo vessel (vehicle carrier),
  ro-ro passenger vessel or tanker, the authorized representative of the
  vessel must ensure that the requirements of regulation 20 of Annex VI
  to MARPOL are met if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the vessel is a new vessel; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the vessel is an existing vessel and is considered
    to be a newly constructed vessel for the purposes of chapter 4 of
    Annex VI to MARPOL.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Required Energy Efficiency Design Index

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} In the case of a vessel of 400 gross tonnage or more
  that is a bulk carrier, combination carrier, container vessel, gas
  carrier, general cargo vessel, refrigerated cargo carrier or tanker,
  the authorized representative of the vessel must ensure that the
  requirements of regulation 21 of Annex VI to MARPOL are met if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the vessel is a new vessel; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the vessel is an existing vessel and is considered
    to be a newly constructed vessel for the purposes of chapter 4 of
    Annex VI to MARPOL.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Waivers

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} In the case of a foreign vessel, the requirements of
  subsections (3) and (4) are subject to the exercise of the power
  conferred by regulation 19.4 of Annex VI to MARPOL by the government
  of the state whose flag the vessel is entitled to fly.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2013-68, s. 19
:::

#### [SUBDIVISION 2]{.HLabel3}[Smoke]{.HTitleText3} {#h-791399 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

- **[[117]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-117 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} This
  Subdivision applies only in respect of vessels in Canadian waters
  within 1 nautical mile from shore.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} This Subdivision does not apply in respect of vessels
  during the start-up or maintenance of smoke-producing systems.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Density of black smoke

- **[[118]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-118 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purposes of this section and section 119, the smoke chart to be
  used in determining the density of black smoke is the Department of
  Transport Smoke Chart set out in Schedule 3 or a comparable chart on
  which fine black dots or lines, evenly spaced on a white ground space,
  are so arranged as to indicate

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} density number 1, by having approximately 20% of
    the space black;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} density number 2, by having approximately 40% of
    the space black;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} density number 3, by having approximately 60% of
    the space black;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} density number 4, by having approximately 80% of
    the space black; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} density number 5, by having approximately 100% of
    the space black.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Visual observation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The density of black smoke is to be determined by
  visual observation, by

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} holding a smoke chart at arm's length;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} viewing the smoke at approximately right angles to
    the line of travel of the smoke; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} matching the shade of the smoke to the shade of
    smoke density that it most closely resembles on the smoke chart.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Closest resemblance

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} When a determination of the density of black smoke is
  made in accordance with subsections (1) and (2), the black smoke is
  considered to be of the density and to have the density number
  indicated by the shade of smoke density that it most closely resembles
  on the smoke chart.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other smoke

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Smoke that is not black smoke is considered to be of
  the same density and to have the same density number as black smoke
  that is of approximately the same degree of opacity.

- Definition of [[black smoke]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} In this section, [[black smoke]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}
  means smoke that appears black or blackish.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limits of smoke emission --- general

- **[[119]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-119
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), a person
  must not operate on a vessel a fuel-burning installation that does not
  utilize hand-fired boilers and that is emitting smoke of a density
  greater than density number 1.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Relaxation of limit

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A fuel-burning installation that does not utilize
  hand-fired boilers may emit smoke of density number 2 for an aggregate
  of not more than 4 minutes in any 30-minute period.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limits of smoke emission --- hand-fired
  boilers

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (4), a person must not operate
  on a vessel a fuel-burning installation that utilizes hand-fired
  boilers and that is emitting smoke of a density greater than density
  number 2.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Relaxation of limit

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A fuel-burning installation that utilizes hand-fired
  boilers may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} while in the Detroit River, emit smoke of a density
    not greater than density number 3 for an aggregate of not more than
    9 minutes in any 30-minute period; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} while elsewhere than in the Detroit River, emit
    smoke

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} of a density not greater than density number 3
      for an aggregate of not more than 9 minutes in any 30-minute
      period, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} of a density not greater than density number 4
      for an aggregate of not more than 3 minutes in any 30-minute
      period.

#### [SUBDIVISION 3]{.HLabel3}[Certificates]{.HTitleText3} {#h-791437 .Subheading}

##### [Certificates, Endorsements and Inspections]{.HTitleText4} {#h-791438 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Issuance of Canadian Air Pollution
Prevention Certificates

- **[[120]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-120 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On
  application by the authorized representative of a Canadian vessel or a
  Canadian pleasure craft, and subject to paragraphs 16(4)(b) to (d) of
  the Act, the Minister must issue a Canadian Air Pollution Prevention
  Certificate to the vessel if the applicable requirements of this
  Division, other than those of section 116.2, are met.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Issuance of International Air Pollution
  Prevention Certificates

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On application by the authorized representative of a
  Canadian vessel or a Canadian pleasure craft, and subject to
  paragraphs 16(4)(b) to (d) of the Act, the Minister must issue an
  International Air Pollution Prevention Certificate to the vessel if
  the applicable requirements of chapter 3 of Annex VI to MARPOL are
  met.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Issuance of International Energy
  Efficiency Certificate

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} On application by the authorized representative of a
  Canadian vessel or a Canadian pleasure craft, and subject to
  paragraphs 16(4)(b) to (d) of the Act, the Minister must issue an
  International Energy Efficiency Certificate to the vessel if the
  applicable requirements of chapter 4 of Annex VI to MARPOL are met.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2013-68, s. 20
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-120-20120330.html "Link to previous version of section 120"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Endorsement of Canadian Air Pollution
Prevention Certificates

- **[[121]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-121 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  authorized representative of a vessel that holds a Canadian Air
  Pollution Prevention Certificate must ensure that the certificate is
  endorsed by the Minister, within three months before or after each
  anniversary date of the issuance of the certificate, to indicate that
  the requirements for the issuance of the certificate are met.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Endorsement of International Air
  Pollution Prevention Certificates

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The authorized representative of a vessel that holds
  an International Air Pollution Prevention Certificate must ensure that
  the certificate is endorsed as required by regulations 5.1.3, 5.1.4
  and 6.3 of Annex VI to MARPOL.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inspection

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the construction, arrangement, equipment,
  fittings, installations or systems of a vessel that holds a
  certificate issued under section 120 are changed as a result of an
  accident, the discovery of a defect, a repair or a major conversion
  that affects the requirements that were met when the certificate was
  issued, the authorized representative of the vessel must ensure that
  the Minister inspects the vessel as soon as feasible to ensure that
  the requirements continue to be met.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (3) does not apply in respect of minor
  repairs or the direct replacement of equipment or fittings that meet
  the requirements of the certificate.

#### [SUBDIVISION 4]{.HLabel3}[Shipboard Documents]{.HTitleText3} {#h-791457 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificates, etc.

- **[[122]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-122
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every vessel of 400 gross tonnage or
  more must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} hold and keep on board

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a Canadian Air Pollution Prevention Certificate,
      or an International Air Pollution Prevention Certificate in the
      form set out in appendix I to Annex VI to MARPOL, if the vessel is
      a Canadian vessel or a Canadian pleasure craft and engages only on
      voyages in waters under Canadian jurisdiction or in the Great
      Lakes and St. Lawrence waters,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} an International Air Pollution Prevention
      Certificate in the form set out in appendix I to Annex VI to
      MARPOL, if the vessel

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} is a Canadian vessel or a Canadian pleasure
        craft and does not engage only on voyages in waters under
        Canadian jurisdiction, or

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} is entitled to fly the flag of a foreign state
        that is a party to Annex VI to MARPOL, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} a certificate of compliance certifying that the
      vessel meets the applicable requirements of Annex VI to MARPOL, if
      the vessel is entitled to fly the flag of a state that is not a
      party to Annex VI to MARPOL; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} keep on board

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} if the vessel has a marine diesel engine in
      respect of which any of sections 110.1 to 110.3 apply, an
      applicable certificate of type approval and a Technical File that
      meets the requirements of section 2.3.4 of the NO~x~ Technical
      Code,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} if the vessel has a shipboard incinerator in
      respect of which section 115 applies, a certificate of type
      approval and an equipment operation manual that specifies how to
      operate the incinerator within the limits set out in paragraph 2
      of appendix IV to Annex VI to MARPOL, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} if the vessel is referred to in subparagraph
      (a)(ii) or (iii), a list, in the form set out in Appendix I to
      Annex VI to MARPOL, of equipment that contains ozone depleting
      substances, other than equipment referred to in subsection 109(5).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}VOC management plan

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every crude oil tanker must keep on board the
  volatile organic compounds management plan referred to in subsection
  112(3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}International Energy Efficiency
  Certificates, etc.

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every vessel of 400 gross tonnage or more must hold
  and keep on board

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an International Energy Efficiency Certificate in
    the form set out in appendix VIII to Annex VI to MARPOL, if the
    vessel

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is a Canadian vessel and does not engage only on
      voyages in waters under Canadian jurisdiction, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} is entitled to fly the flag of a foreign state
      that is a party to Annex VI to MARPOL; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a certificate of compliance certifying that the
    vessel meets the applicable requirements of Annex VI to MARPOL, if
    the vessel is entitled to fly the flag of a state that is not a
    party to Annex VI to MARPOL.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ship Energy Efficiency Management Plans

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Every vessel of 400 gross tonnage or more that does
  not engage only on voyages in waters under Canadian jurisdiction, and
  every Canadian vessel of 400 gross tonnage or more that engages only
  on voyages in waters under Canadian jurisdiction, must keep on board a
  Ship Energy Efficiency Management Plan that meets the requirements of
  regulation 22 of Annex VI to MARPOL. The Plan may form part of the
  vessel's Safety Management System, if the vessel has one.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application --- U.S. vessels

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Subsections (3) and (4) do not apply in respect of a
  vessel that is entitled to fly the flag of the United States.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2013-68, s. 21
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-122-20120330.html "Link to previous version of section 122"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [SUBDIVISION 5]{.HLabel3}[Record-keeping and Samples]{.HTitleText3} {#h-791485 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Record book of engine parameters

**[[123]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-123 .sectionLabel}** A vessel that is fitted
with a marine diesel engine in respect of which any of sections 110.1 to
110.3 apply must keep on board a record book of engine parameters and
maintain it in accordance with section 6.2.2 of the NO~x~ Technical
Code.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2013-68, s. 22
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-123-20120330.html "Link to previous version of section 123"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Bunker delivery notes

- **[[124]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-124 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  master of a vessel referred to in subparagraph 122(1)(a)(ii) or (iii)
  must ensure that the details of fuel oil delivered to and used on
  board the vessel for combustion purposes are recorded in a bunker
  delivery note that contains at least the information specified in
  appendix V to Annex VI to MARPOL.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Keep on board

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The vessel's master must keep the bunker delivery
  note on board for three years after the day on which the fuel oil was
  delivered on board.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fuel oil samples

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The vessel's master must ensure that the requirements
  of regulation 18.8.1 of Annex VI to MARPOL, respecting a
  representative sample of the fuel oil delivered that must accompany
  the bunker delivery note, are met.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2013-68, s. 23
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-124-20120330.html "Link to previous version of section 124"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ozone Depleting Substances Record Book

- **[[124.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-124.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every vessel referred to in
  subparagraph 122(1)(a)(ii) or (iii) that has a rechargeable system
  containing ozone depleting substances, other than any system or
  equipment referred to in subsection 109(5), must maintain an Ozone
  Depleting Substances Record Book and keep it on board.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Entries

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The vessel's master must ensure that entries are made
  without delay in the Ozone Depleting Substances Record Book in respect
  of the following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the repair or maintenance of equipment containing
    ozone depleting substances;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the recharge, full or partial, of equipment
    containing ozone depleting substances;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any emission of ozone depleting substances;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the transfer of ozone depleting substances to
    land-based reception facilities; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the supply of ozone depleting substances to the
    vessel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Mass of ozone depleting substances

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The entries must include the mass of the ozone
  depleting substances that are involved in a recharge of equipment or
  that are emitted, transferred or supplied, as the case may be.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2013-68, s. 24
:::

#### [SUBDIVISION 6]{.HLabel3}[Exemptions and Equivalents]{.HTitleText3} {#h-791513 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Board

- **[[125]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-125 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Board may, in respect of Canadian vessels and Canadian pleasure craft,
  exercise the powers of the Administration conferred by regulations 3.2
  and 4 of Annex VI to MARPOL.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Foreign governments

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In the case of a foreign vessel or a foreign pleasure
  craft, the requirements of this Division are subject to the exercise
  of the powers conferred by regulations 3.2 and 4 of Annex VI to MARPOL
  by the government of the state whose flag the vessel is entitled to
  fly.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2013-68, s. 24
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-125-20120330.html "Link to previous version of section 125"){.PITLink}
:::

### [DIVISION 7]{.HLabel2}[Pollutant Substances]{.HTitleText2} {#h-791521 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Discharge prohibited

- **[[126]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-126 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  vessel in waters under Canadian jurisdiction, and a person on such a
  vessel, must not discharge, except in accordance with subsection (2)
  or in the circumstances set out in section 5 that apply in respect of
  the discharge, a substance listed in Schedule 1 that is not

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} carried in packaged form; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} carried in a cargo container, a road vehicle, a
    trailer, a portable tank, a railway vehicle or a tank mounted on a
    chassis.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authorized discharge --- noxious liquid
  substances

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (1), a noxious liquid
  substance may be discharged from a vessel in Section II waters if the
  discharge is made in accordance with any of sections 68 to 71.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authorized discharge --- chlorine

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (1), chlorine that is
  in sewage effluent may be discharged from a vessel if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the discharge is made as a result of the chlorine
    being used in a marine sanitation device to disinfect sewage; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} when the vessel is in Section I waters or at a
    distance of less than three nautical miles from shore in Section II
    waters, the total residual chlorine content in the effluent is equal
    to or less than 0.5 mg/L.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limited application --- pleasure craft

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} This section applies in respect of pleasure craft
  that are not Canadian vessels only when they are in Canadian waters.

### [DIVISION 8]{.HLabel2}[Anti-fouling Systems]{.HTitleText2} {#h-791535 .Subheading}

#### [Controls on Anti-fouling Systems]{.HTitleText3} {#h-791536 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Organotin compounds

- **[[127]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-127 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  authorized representative of a vessel must ensure that it does not
  have an anti-fouling system that contains any organotin compound that
  acts as a biocide.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}If applied before January 1, 2008

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply in respect of an
  organotin compound applied before January 1, 2008 that has a coating
  that forms a barrier preventing the compound from leaching.

#### [Certificates and Endorsements]{.HTitleText3} {#h-791542 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Issuance of International Anti-fouling
System Certificates

**[[128]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-128 .sectionLabel}** On application by the
authorized representative of a Canadian vessel or a Canadian pleasure
craft and subject to paragraphs 16(4)(b) to (d) of the Act, the Minister
must issue an International Anti-fouling System Certificate to the
vessel if the applicable requirements of Annex 1 to the Anti-fouling
Systems Convention are met.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Endorsement

**[[129]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-129 .sectionLabel}** The authorized
representative of a vessel that holds an International Anti-fouling
System Certificate must ensure that the certificate is endorsed as
required by regulation 1(1)(b) of Annex 4 to the Anti-fouling Systems
Convention.

#### [Shipboard Documents]{.HTitleText3} {#h-791547 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificates

**[[130]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-130 .sectionLabel}** Every vessel of
400 gross tonnage or more must hold and keep on board

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} an International Anti-fouling System Certificate in
  the form set out in appendix 1 to Annex 4 to the Anti-fouling Systems
  Convention, if the vessel

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is a Canadian vessel or a Canadian pleasure craft,
    or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} is entitled to fly the flag of a foreign state
    that is a party to the Anti-fouling Systems Convention; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a certificate of compliance certifying that the
  vessel meets the applicable requirements of the Anti-fouling Systems
  Convention, if the vessel is entitled to fly the flag of a state that
  is not a party to the Anti-fouling Systems Convention.

Definition of [[length]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

- **[[131]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-131 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  this section, [[length]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as
  in article 2(8) of the International Convention on Load Lines, 1966,
  as modified by the Protocol of 1988 relating to the Convention.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Anti-fouling Systems Declaration

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A vessel that is 24 m or more in length but of less
  than 400 gross tonnage and does not engage only on voyages in waters
  under Canadian jurisdiction must keep on board a declaration
  confirming that the anti-fouling system applied to the vessel meets
  the applicable requirements of Annex 1 to the Anti-fouling Systems
  Convention.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Signing

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The declaration must be signed by the authorized
  representative, in the case of a Canadian vessel, and the owner, in
  the case of any other vessel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Language

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The declaration must be in the form set out in
  Schedule 4. It must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a Canadian vessel or a Canadian
    pleasure craft, be written in English or French; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a foreign vessel or a foreign
    pleasure craft, be written in English, French or Spanish.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Endorsement

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The declaration must be accompanied by appropriate
  documentation, such as a paint receipt or a contractor invoice, or
  contain an appropriate endorsement of the system applied to the
  vessel.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2013-68, s. 25(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-131-20120330.html "Link to previous version of section 131"){.PITLink}
:::

### [DIVISION 9]{.HLabel2}[Greywater]{.HTitleText2} {#h-791569 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

- **[[131.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-131.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The following definitions apply in
  this section.

  [[greywater]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[greywater]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means drainage from sinks,
      laundry machines, bath tubs, shower-stalls or dishwashers. It does
      not include sewage, or drainage from machinery spaces or workshop
      areas. ([eaux grises]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[new passenger vessel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[new passenger vessel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a passenger vessel that is constructed on or
        after the day on which this section comes into force;

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a passenger vessel that, on or after the day on
        which this section comes into force, undergoes a conversion that

        - [(i)]{.lawlabel} substantially alters the dimensions or
          carrying capacity of the vessel, or

        - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} is intended to substantially prolong the
          life of the vessel; or

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a vessel that, on or after the day on which
        this section comes into force, is converted into a passenger
        vessel. ([bâtiment à passagers neuf]{.DefinedTermLink
        lang="fr"})

  [[release]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[release]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes spilling, leaking,
      pumping, pouring, emitting, emptying, throwing and
      dumping. ([libération]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} This section applies in respect of vessels in waters
  under Canadian jurisdiction other than arctic waters.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} This section does not apply in respect of a release
  of greywater that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is necessary for the purpose of saving lives,
    securing the safety of a vessel or preventing the immediate loss of
    a vessel; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} occurs as a result of an accident of navigation in
    which a vessel or its equipment is damaged, unless the accident
    occurs as a result of an action that is outside the ordinary
    practice of seafarers.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Solids in water and sheen on water
  prohibited

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The authorized representative of a vessel must ensure
  that any release of greywater by or from the vessel into the water
  does not result in the deposit of solids in the water or leave a sheen
  on the water.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}New passenger vessels

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The authorized representative of a new passenger
  vessel that is carrying more than 500 passengers must ensure that any
  release of greywater by or from the vessel into the water

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is passed through a marine sanitation device that
    meets the requirements of section 90; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is made at a distance of at least three nautical
    miles from shore.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificates of type approval

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Every vessel that is fitted with a marine sanitation
  device in order to meet the requirements of paragraph (5)(a) must keep
  on board

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a certificate of type approval

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a device referred to in subsection
      90(1), certifying that the device meets the applicable
      requirements referred to in that subsection, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a device referred to in
      subsection 90(2), certifying that the device was approved as an
      approved device under the [Great Lakes Sewage Pollution Prevention
      Regulations](/eng/regulations/C.R.C.,_c._1429) and bearing the
      approval number; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a manual that sets out the operational and
    maintenance procedures for the device.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2013-68, s. 26
:::

## [PART 3]{.HLabel1}[Pollutant Discharge Reporting]{.HTitleText1} {#h-791601 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Vessels in waters under Canadian
jurisdiction

- **[[132]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-132 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  master of a vessel in waters under Canadian jurisdiction must report
  any discharge or anticipated discharge from the vessel if the
  discharge or anticipated discharge is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} prohibited by section 187 of the Act or by these
    Regulations; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} authorized by paragraph 5(a), (b), (d) or (e).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Canadian vessels in other waters

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The master of a Canadian vessel in waters that are
  not waters under Canadian jurisdiction must report any discharge or
  anticipated discharge from the vessel of oil, a noxious liquid
  substance carried in bulk or a marine pollutant that is not carried in
  bulk if the discharge or anticipated discharge is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} prohibited by section 187 of the Act or by these
    Regulations; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} authorized by paragraph 5(a), (b), (d) or (e).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When report is to be made

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The master must make the report

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} as soon as a discharge occurs or is anticipated; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} as soon as feasible after a discharge occurs or is
    anticipated, if the master is unable to make the report under
    paragraph (a) because he or she is involved in activities relating
    to

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} saving lives,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} securing the vessel's safety or preventing its
      immediate loss,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} preventing or mitigating damage to the vessel
      or its equipment, or

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} preventing or mitigating damage to the
      environment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}If report not made in accordance with
  paragraph (3)(a)

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the authorized representative of a Canadian
  vessel, or the owner of any other vessel, is not on board the vessel
  and has knowledge that a report has not been made in accordance with
  paragraph (3)(a), the authorized representative or owner must make the
  report immediately.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of report

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Every report must be made in accordance with
  sections 2 and 3.1 to 3.3 of the appendix to the Annex to the General
  Principles for Ship Reporting Systems and Ship Reporting Requirements,
  Including Guidelines for Reporting Incidents Involving Dangerous
  Goods, Harmful Substances and/or Marine Pollutants, IMO
  Resolution A.851(20), and must include the following information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the identity of every vessel involved;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the date, time and location of the discharge or the
    estimated date, time and location of the anticipated discharge;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the nature of the discharge or anticipated
    discharge, including the type and estimated quantity of pollutant
    involved; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a discharge, a description of the
    assistance and salvage measures employed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Vessels rendering assistance or
  undertaking salvage

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The master of a vessel must report the particulars of
  any action taken in rendering assistance to or undertaking salvage of
  another vessel whose master is required under subsection (1) or (2) to
  report a discharge or an anticipated discharge.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}To whom reports are made

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} A report required by subsection (1), (2), (4) or (6)
  must be made to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a marine safety inspector or a marine
    communications and traffic services officer, in the case of a
    discharge or anticipated discharge in waters under Canadian
    jurisdiction; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an appropriate official of the nearest coastal
    state, in the case of a discharge or anticipated discharge from a
    Canadian vessel in waters that are not waters under Canadian
    jurisdiction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Supplementary reports

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} A person who makes a report must, whenever there is
  further information relating to the incident and essential for the
  protection of the marine environment, submit to the person to whom the
  report was made a supplementary report with as much of that
  information as possible.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Language

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} Despite the requirement in the IMO Resolution
  referred to in subsection (5) that the languages used in reports
  include English where language difficulties may exist, a report made
  to a marine safety inspector or a marine communications and traffic
  services officer may be made in English or French.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Oil handling facilities

- **[[133]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-133 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an oil handling facility who is required to have an oil
  pollution emergency plan under paragraph 168(1)(d) of the Act must, as
  soon as feasible,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} report any discharge or anticipated discharge of
    oil to the federal emergency telephone number identified in the oil
    pollution emergency plan; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} report in writing any discharge or anticipated
    discharge of oil to the Department of Transport Marine Safety Office
    nearest to the facility.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of report

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The report must include the following information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the identity of any vessel involved;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the name and address of the oil handling facility;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the name and position of the person who is
    responsible for implementing and coordinating the oil pollution
    emergency plan;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the date, time and location of the discharge or the
    estimated date, time and location of the anticipated discharge;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the nature of the discharge or anticipated
    discharge, including the type and estimated quantity of oil
    involved;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} a description of the response actions to be taken;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} on-scene conditions; and

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} any other relevant information.

## [PART 4]{.HLabel1}[Consequential Amendment, Repeals and Coming into Force]{.HTitleText1} {#h-791652 .Part}

### [Consequential Amendment to the Vessel Clearance Regulations]{.HTitleText2} {#h-791653 .Subheading}

**[[134]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-134 .sectionLabel}** \[Amendment\]

### [Repeals]{.HTitleText2} {#h-791655 .Subheading}

**[[135]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-135 .sectionLabel}** \[Repeal\]

**[[136]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-136 .sectionLabel}** \[Repeal\]

### [Coming into Force]{.HTitleText2} {#h-791658 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Registration date

**[[137]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-137 .sectionLabel}** These Regulations come
into force on the day on which they are registered.

## RELATED PROVISIONS {#related-provisions .scheduleTitleText}

- --- SOR/2021-60, s. 12

  - [12]{.lawlabel} Sections 61 and 63, subsections 64(2) and (3),
    section 65 and paragraph 68(a) of the [Vessel Pollution and
    Dangerous Chemicals Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2012-69) apply
    in respect of vessels that are capable of engaging in the drilling
    for, or the production, conservation or processing of, oil or gas,
    except when the vessel is on location and engaged in the exploration
    or drilling for, or the production, conservation or processing of,
    [[oil]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} or [[gas]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} as
    defined in section 2 of the [Canada Oil and Gas Operations
    Act](/eng/acts/O-7), in an area described in paragraph 3(a) or (b)
    of that Act.
